## 2026-05-08 - API-0381
- **Implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for the
  `settings-readTraefikFile` Dokploy API operation (GET
  /settings.readTraefikFile, tag settings). Added a single
  `apiCoverageCase` entry to `coveredAPIOperations` so the
  registry-presence, schema-reachability, manifest-inclusion,
  httptest success-leg, and 401 -> CodeAuth failure-leg subtests
  fire automatically. The operation declares **two query
  parameters** -- REQUIRED `path` (string, the Traefik
  configuration filename) and OPTIONAL `serverId` (string,
  multi-node scope) -- and no request body. Both are populated in
  `SampleQuery` with unique `set-cov-readTraefikFile-0381-*`
  fixture slugs so the success leg's `r.URL.Query()` re-read
  exercises end-to-end forwarding for the full parameter surface.
  This is the **first settings/* GET to declare a REQUIRED query
  parameter** -- every prior parameter-bearing peer in the
  settings/* GET cohort (API-0369, API-0373, API-0377, API-0380)
  carried only an OPTIONAL `serverId`.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` -- appended the API-0381
    case literal to the `coveredAPIOperations` slice.
  - `ralph/prd.json` -- set API-0381 `passes: true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` -- this entry.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` -- clean.
  - `go mod tidy` -- clean.
  - `go vet ./...` -- clean.
  - `go test ./...` -- all packages pass; `internal/cli` runs the
    new `API-0381/settings-readTraefikFile` success and failure
    subtests end-to-end through the production exit-code path.
  - `go test -race ./...` -- all packages pass.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`,
  `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - The harness still does not gate on `required`-ness -- it
    forwards whatever `SampleQuery` carries. That is the
    intentional contract (the executor is a thin pass-through and
    Dokploy's upstream handler enforces required-ness), but a
    future story that wants to assert REQUIRED-parameter
    enforcement at the executor layer will need a dedicated
    sub-suite outside the coverage harness.
  - 400 (CodeInvalidInput) is the operation's most distinguishing
    failure mode given the REQUIRED `path` parameter (Dokploy
    rejects malformed paths or paths that escape the allowed
    Traefik configuration directory), but the coverage harness
    keeps the canonical 401 -> CodeAuth representative for parity
    with every prior settings/* GET entry. A future story that
    wants 400-specific assertions for this operation can override
    `FailureStatus`/`FailureCode` explicitly without touching
    the harness.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The `required` axis is its own dimension on the parameter
    sub-axis -- even when two adjacent parameter-bearing settings/*
    GETs both declare a single query parameter, one can be
    REQUIRED and the other OPTIONAL. API-0381 is the first
    settings/* GET to carry a REQUIRED query parameter; the
    immediately preceding peer API-0380 carried only an OPTIONAL
    `serverId`. Direct spec inspection at
    `/settings.readTraefikFile > get > parameters` is the only
    way to surface this -- slug-prefix and parameter-count
    similarity are not sufficient signals.
  - When an operation declares MULTIPLE query parameters,
    populate ALL of them in `SampleQuery` (not just the
    REQUIRED ones) so the success-leg `r.URL.Query()` re-read
    exercises end-to-end forwarding for the full parameter
    surface. The harness ignores empty maps but does iterate
    every key the case provides, so a partial `SampleQuery`
    silently skips the missing-key forwarding assertion.
  - The fixture slug convention extends naturally to
    multi-parameter cases: append a `-<param>` suffix to the
    canonical `<tag-prefix>-cov-<opID>-<storyID>` slug
    (`set-cov-readTraefikFile-0381-path` and
    `set-cov-readTraefikFile-0381-server`) so each parameter
    has its own unique literal. The hyphenated suffix keeps the
    fixture-namespace lookup grep-friendly across the test file.
  - The next case in the settings/* roster per PRD ordering is
    API-0382 `settings-readWebServerTraefikConfig`. The
    forward-reference lesson (parameter axis, response-set axis,
    request-body axis) reasserted at API-0345..API-0381 still
    applies -- the next contributor must re-verify against
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.readWebServerTraefikConfig > get` before assuming
    any field is identical to this entry. The `read*` slug
    family continues to straddle every sub-axis.

    sub-axis added by API-0381 stays a distinct dimension; future
    parameter-bearing entries must not assume OPTIONAL just

    REQUIRED.

    continues to require ZERO production code changes -- only the
    `apiCoverageCase` literal is needed because the harness's
    success leg defaults `SuccessResponse` to `{}` and the

    minimal-literal pattern.

    (GET -> POST) and is the second POST on the settings/* roster
    after API-0370 `getUpdateData`. The `reload*` slug family
    (`reloadRedis`, `reloadServer`, `reloadTraefik` per the
    PRD priority list) is a mutating action family, so the
    parameter and request-body axes are likely different from the
    `read*` reader family. Direct spec inspection at
    `/settings.reloadRedis > post` is mandatory -- the response
    set, parameter list, and request body must each be confirmed
    rather than inherited.

- Implemented coverage entry for `bitbucket-update` (POST /bitbucket.update) — the seventh and final entry on the bitbucket/* coverage roster, closing the bitbucket/* tag arc opened at API-0051. New `apiCoverageCase` literal slotted between API-0056 `bitbucket-testConnection` and API-0066 `compose-cancelDeployment` in the append-only `coveredAPIOperations` registry.
- Files changed:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended one `apiCoverageCase` for API-0057 with full design-rationale header (~120 comment lines covering: tag-arc closing, per-tag namespace inheritance from API-0051..API-0056, spec re-verification per API-0345..API-0364 forward-reference lesson, required-triple `bitbucketId` + `gitProviderId` + `name` distinct from API-0052's required-pair, workspace-field-name catch (`bitbucketWorkspaceName` here vs `workspaceName` at API-0056), no-404 reservation honored from API-0055, family-choice consumption of the harness-default 401 → CodeAuth slot pre-reserved by API-0056's forward-reference, fixture conventions consuming the `bb-cov-update-0057` slug pre-reserved by API-0056's design-rationale header).

  - `gofmt -w .`
  - `goimports -w .`
  - `go mod tidy`
  - `go vet ./...` → clean.
  - `go test ./...` → all packages pass (internal/cli covers in 1.255s).
  - `go test -race ./...` → all packages pass (internal/cli covers in 2.691s).
- Results: bitbucket/* tag is now fully covered (API-0051 through API-0057, all 7 stories `passes: true`). The `coveredAPIOperations` harness automatically exercises registry presence (method/path/tag), `yalla api call bitbucket-update --input request.json --json` success-leg envelope (`yalla.output.v1`, `data.method`/`data.status`), `yalla schema get bitbucket-update --json` reachability, manifest inclusion, representative-failure leg (401 → CodeAuth via harness default), token redaction, and `--no-input` non-interactivity for this entry.
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none specific to API-0057. The bitbucket/* tag arc is closed; no forward-reference reservation was opened for future bitbucket/* peers because none remain in the PRD. The next pending story is API-0066 `compose-cancelDeployment` (already covered, `passes: true`) — the next *unflagged* story per priority order should be looked up from `prd.json`.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Per-tag arc-closing entries should explicitly state the closure.** When the entry being added is the last pending story in its tag (verified via `prd.json` filter on `apiOperation.tag`), the design-rationale header should call this out and explicitly *not* open a forward-reference reservation for future peers, mirroring the inverse of API-0051..API-0055's "Future contributors picking up..." paragraphs. This keeps the breadcrumb trail honest — the next contributor reading API-0057's header should not search for a phantom "API-0058 bitbucket-*" continuation.
  - **Update vs create body-shape divergence is a per-operation re-verification target.** API-0052 `bitbucket-create` requires `authId` + `name` (allocates a fresh `bitbucketId` under an `authId`); API-0057 `bitbucket-update` requires `bitbucketId` + `gitProviderId` + `name` (binds an existing `bitbucketId` and `gitProviderId`). The two POSTs share neither their required-set nor their full optional-set (`organizationId` is unique to update; `authId` is unique to create). Future `*-update` peers should grep this entry for the precedent.
  - **Workspace-field-name catch.** Within the same tag, the workspace identifier oscillates between `bitbucketWorkspaceName` (used at API-0052 `bitbucket-create` and API-0057 `bitbucket-update`) and `workspaceName` (used at API-0056 `bitbucket-testConnection`). Per-operation re-verification is mandatory; copying the field name from a sibling without re-checking the spec would silently break payload validation. The API-0353..API-0362 settings/* `clean*` body-axis reminder (originally documented at API-0055) generalizes to this pattern.
  - **Harness-default 401 → CodeAuth pre-reservation pattern.** API-0056's design-rationale header explicitly forward-referenced API-0057 ("expected to follow the same harness-default convention"). That reservation was honored verbatim — the entry sets neither `FailureStatus` nor `FailureCode`, falling through to the harness default. Future contributors writing forward-references should make them as concrete as API-0056 did (specific operation ID + specific failure-code expectation), not vague hints, so the next iteration can consume the slot without re-derivation.
  - **Per-tag namespace exhaustion.** The bitbucket/* `bb-cov-*` namespace is now closed (slugs: `bb-cov-providers-0051`, `bb-cov-create-0052-*`, `bb-cov-getBranches-0053-*`, `bb-cov-getRepos-0054-*`, `bb-cov-one-0055-*`, `bb-cov-testConn-0056-*`, `bb-cov-update-0057-*`). No future story may write into `bb-cov-*` per the per-tag isolation rule established at API-0246 and reasserted by the API-0051 bitbucket/* kickoff header.

  passive readers to `reload*` mutating actions. It is the
  second POST on the settings/* roster after API-0370
  `getUpdateData` broke the eleven-entry POST opener -> GET
  streak.

- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`,
  `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - 291 PRD stories still have `passes: false`; next in the
    settings/* roster per PRD ordering is API-0384
    `settings-reloadServer` -- a sibling `reload*` POST that
    direct spec inspection confirms is structurally identical
    to API-0383 (no `requestBody`, zero parameters,
    200/400/401/403/500). The next contributor must still
    re-verify against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.reloadServer > post` per the forward-reference
    lesson; the parameter, request-body, response-set, and
    verb axes must each be re-verified per-operation rather
    than inherited from this entry.
  - API-0385 `settings-reloadTraefik` is the third `reload*`
    peer and is already known from preliminary spec inspection
    to declare an OPTIONAL `requestBody` with an OPTIONAL
    `serverId` field -- the first `reload*` peer to break the
    no-body shape. The forward-reference in API-0383's
    in-source design-rationale flags this so the API-0384/0385
    contributor does not blindly inherit from this entry.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **No-body POST shape is now the dominant settings/*
    mutating-action shape.** Six of the settings/* POSTs
    covered so far (API-0354 `cleanAllDeploymentQueue`,
    API-0357 `cleanMonitoring`, API-0358 `cleanRedis`,
    API-0359 `cleanSSHPrivateKey`, API-0370 `getUpdateData`,
    and now API-0383 `reloadRedis`) declare no `requestBody`
    field at all. This is structurally distinct from the
    optional-body POSTs (API-0351 `assignDomainServer`,
    API-0353 `cancelQueues`, API-0355 `cleanGitProvider`,
    API-0356 `cleanLogs`, API-0360 `cleanSwarm`, API-0361
    `cleanTemporaryLogs`, API-0362 `cleanUnusedVolumes`).
    Both shapes share the same 200/400/401/403/500 response
    set (no 404); only the request-body axis differs.
    Future settings/* POST contributors should expect roughly
    half of the roster to be no-body and the other half to be
    optional-body, per the spec.
  - **`reload*` slug prefix is a semantic-family hint, not a
    contract guarantee.** API-0383 `reloadRedis` and
    (predicted) API-0384 `reloadServer` are no-body POSTs;
    API-0385 `reloadTraefik` is an optional-body POST with
    an optional `serverId` field. The same `reload*` family
    therefore straddles the request-body axis. The
    slug-prefix-is-not-shape rule reasserted at
    API-0371..API-0382 still holds; `reload*` is no exception.
  - **The verb-axis flip-flop after a long GET cohort is now
    a recurring pattern.** API-0370 broke an eleven-entry POST
    opener (API-0351..API-0362) -> seven-entry GET cohort
    (API-0363..API-0369) with a single POST, then the GET
    cohort resumed (API-0371..API-0382, twelve entries),
    then API-0383 broke it again with a POST. The
    settings/* roster therefore has at least three verb
    pivots so far. Per-operation re-verification stays the
    only safe way to determine the next entry's verb; do not
    assume verb continuity even within a same-prefix slug
    family.
  - **`reload*` actions are platform-wide, not per-resource.**
    Like the `clean*` family, the `reload*` family operates
    on a controller-owned global service (Redis, the Dokploy
    server itself, Traefik) rather than a per-tenant resource;
    this is why 404 is absent from the response set
    (there is no per-resource lookup that could miss). The
    optional `serverId` on `reloadTraefik` (API-0385)
    optionally scopes the reload to a remote node but does
    not make the operation per-resource in the
    has-an-id-and-can-404 sense. Future `reload*` contributors
    should not introduce 404 -> CodeNotFound coverage for
    this family.
  - **The empty-body, parameter-free, no-`requestBody` POST
    shape requires ZERO production code changes -- only the
    `apiCoverageCase` literal is needed, exactly mirroring
    the API-0370 minimal-literal pattern. The harness's POST
    branch with `len(SampleBody) == 0` skips the
    `Content-Type` and body-round-trip assertions per the
    `if len(tc.SampleBody) > 0` guard inside
    `runAPICoverageSuccess`, and the failure leg defaults to
    401 -> CodeAuth when `FailureStatus == 0`.
---
## 2026-05-08 - API-0058
- **Implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for the
  `certificates-all` Dokploy API operation
  (GET /certificates.all, tag certificates). Appended a single
  `apiCoverageCase` entry to `coveredAPIOperations` (slotted
  between API-0057 `bitbucket-update` and API-0066
  `compose-cancelDeployment` to honor the StoryID-sorted
  diff-friendliness rule documented at the top of
  `coveredAPIOperations`). The harness fires registry-presence
  (method/path/tag), `yalla schema get certificates-all --json`
  reachability, manifest inclusion, an httptest success-leg
  (200 / `{}`), and a 401 -> CodeAuth representative-failure
  leg (harness defaults — `FailureStatus == 0`,
  `FailureCode == ""`).
- **Spec axes** (re-verified directly against
  `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /certificates.all > get`):
  GET, **zero parameters** (no path placeholders, no query
  string), **no requestBody**, response set
  200/400/401/403/404/500. The 200 schema is `{}` with
  `additionalProperties: false`. Body is unauthenticated-shaped
  except for the `Authorization` security requirement.
- **Tag-arc opener**: API-0058 opens the certificates/* tag
  arc. Three peers remain pending: API-0059
  `certificates-create`, API-0060 `certificates-one`, API-0061
  `certificates-remove`. The fresh `cert-cov-*` fixture-isolation
  namespace is opened nominally — no literals consumed because
  this entry has no `Sample*` fields. Forward-reference
  reservation: API-0060 `certificates-one` is the canonical
  by-id reader and should claim the 404 -> CodeNotFound
  representative-failure slot (mirroring API-0055
  `bitbucket-one`'s claim of that slot in the bitbucket/* tag
  arc); the API-0060 contributor must still re-verify against
  `/certificates.one > get` rather than blindly inheriting.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` -- appended the
    API-0058 case literal to the `coveredAPIOperations` slice
    between API-0057 and API-0066, with a
    ~80-line design-rationale comment block.
  - `ralph/prd.json` -- set API-0058 `passes: true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` -- this entry.
- **Verification run** from repo root:
  - `gofmt -w .` -- clean.
  - `go mod tidy` -- clean.
  - `go vet ./...` -- clean.
  - `go test ./...` -- all packages pass; `internal/cli` runs
    the new `API-0058/certificates-all` registry, schema,
    manifest, success-leg, and failure-leg subtests
    end-to-end through the production exit-code path
    (1.145s).
  - `go test -race ./...` -- all packages pass (2.746s).
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`,
  `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - 289 PRD stories still have `passes: false`. The next
    pending priority-5 story is API-0059 `certificates-create`
    (POST /certificates.create) -- a sibling certificates/*
    mutation that should consume a `cert-cov-create-0059-*`
    fixture-token namespace, but per-operation re-verification
    against `/certificates.create > post` is mandatory.
  - API-0060 `certificates-one` is the canonical by-id reader
    in the certificates/* tag and should claim the 404 ->
    CodeNotFound representative-failure slot reserved by this
    entry's design-rationale header.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Minimal-literal GET shape is the canonical opener for a
    list-style tag arc.** When a tag's first pending entry is
    a parameter-free, body-free GET (e.g. `<tag>-all` /
    `<tag>-list`), the case literal should consist of nothing
    but `StoryID`, `OperationID`, `Method`, `Path`, `Tag`, and
    a design-rationale comment block — every `Sample*` field
    omitted, defaulting to 200 / `{}` for success and 401 ->
    CodeAuth for failure. This matches the minimal-literal
    pattern established at API-0378/API-0379 and most
    recently consumed by API-0383 `settings-reloadRedis`. The
    no-input-flag path is exercised because
    `buildCoverageInputArgs` short-circuits to an empty slice
    when no `Sample*` fields are populated.
  - **Tag-arc-opener entries must explicitly nominate a 404 ->
    CodeNotFound holder.** When opening a new tag arc whose
    PRD priority list contains both a list-GET (`<tag>-all`)
    and a by-id-GET (`<tag>-one`), the list-GET opener must
    explicitly reserve the 404 -> CodeNotFound
    representative-failure slot for the upcoming
    `<tag>-one` peer (this is exactly what API-0055
    `bitbucket-one` did for the bitbucket/* tag). Reserving
    the slot in the opener — rather than letting it default
    out at the by-id peer — keeps the per-tag failure-leg
    distribution legible and prevents a future
    `<tag>-one` contributor from accidentally consuming the
    harness-default 401 -> CodeAuth slot.
  - **StoryID-sorted insertion catches comment-anchor
    ambiguity.** The `coveredAPIOperations` slice is kept
    sorted by StoryID for diff-friendliness. Inserting
    API-0058 between API-0057 (line 2569) and API-0066
    (line 2570) required matching a unique anchor — the
    bare `SuccessResponse: ` + "`" + "{}" + "`" + `,
	},
	{` pattern
    appears many times in the file, so the edit had to
    include enough of the API-0057 closing context (the
    `bb-cov-update-0057-organizationId` literal and its
    trailing comment block) to disambiguate. Future
    contributors editing into the middle of the slice should
    anchor on the prior entry's distinctive last lines, not
    on the generic struct-closer pattern.
  - **Indentation-of-body-vs-comment trap.** The Read tool's
    `cat -n` format prepends `<lineno>\t` so every line in a
    Read result *appears* to have one extra leading tab.
    The actual struct body uses single-tab indentation
    (`\t` for braces, `\t\t` for fields), not the apparent
    double-tab indentation. Edit calls must strip the Read
    line-number prefix before constructing `old_string` /
    `new_string`. A first attempt with double-tab indentation
    failed with "String to replace not found in file"; the
    fix was to verify the actual byte representation via
    `ctx_execute_file` + `repr(line)` before retrying.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0384 `settings-reloadServer`
- **What was implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for
  Dokploy's `POST /settings.reloadServer` (no-body, no-parameter

  `settings-reloadRedis` and the slice's closing brace, locking
  in the registry-presence, schema, manifest, success-leg, and
  failure-leg subtests in one shot via the existing harness.

  `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /settings.reloadServer >
  post`):
  - Method **POST**, tag **settings**, security
    `Authorization: []` (RequiresAuth=true).
  - **No `requestBody` field at all** — true no-input POST,
    structurally identical to the immediately-prior
    API-0383 `reloadRedis`, and to API-0354/0357/0358/0359/0370.
  - **Zero parameters** (no query, no path, no header).
  - Responses **200 / 400 / 401 / 403 / 500** — note **no
    404**, matching every prior settings/* mutating-POST peer.
  - 200 schema is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`.
- **Case literal** is minimal — only `StoryID`, `OperationID`,
  `Method`, `Path`, `Tag` + comment block. All `Sample*` fields
  omitted; harness defaults handle success (`200` / `{}`) and
  failure (`401` -> `CodeAuth`).
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` -- appended the
    API-0384 case literal to the `coveredAPIOperations` slice
    immediately after API-0383, with a ~125-line
    design-rationale comment block that records the
    spec-re-verification trail, the second-consecutive
    `reload*` no-body shape, the forward-reference
    confirmation against API-0383's hand-off prediction, and
    the forward-reference for API-0385 (verified at
    write-time to declare an OPTIONAL `requestBody` carrying
    an OPTIONAL `serverId` field — the first `reload*` peer
    to break the no-body shape).
  - `ralph/prd.json` -- flipped API-0384 `passes` to `true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` -- this entry.
- **Verification run** from repo root:
  - `gofmt -w .` -- clean.
  - `go mod tidy` -- clean.
  - `go vet ./...` -- clean.
  - `go test ./...` -- all packages pass; `internal/cli` runs
    the new `API-0384/settings-reloadServer` registry,
    schema, manifest, success-leg, and failure-leg subtests
    end-to-end through the production exit-code path.
  - `go test -race ./...` -- all packages pass (2.7s).
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`,
  `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - 288 PRD stories still have `passes: false` (down from
    289). The next pending priority-2 story is API-0385
    `settings-reloadTraefik` (POST /settings.reloadTraefik).
  - API-0385 is the **first** `reload*` peer to break the
    no-body shape: spec inspection at write-time shows it
    declares an OPTIONAL `requestBody` (`required: false`)
    carrying an OPTIONAL `serverId` string field. The
    contributor must decide between leaving `SampleBody`
    unset (relying on `required: false`) or materialising a
    representative `{"serverId":"set-cov-reloadTraefik-0385"}`
    payload to exercise the JSON round-trip — the latter is
    preferred for parity with prior optional-body operations
    covered by the harness.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Two-entry no-body POST run inside `reload*`.** API-0383
    and API-0384 both ship the canonical no-body, no-parameter
    POST shape (the same minimal-literal pattern established
    at API-0378/API-0379 for parameter-free GETs and most
    recently consumed by API-0383). When two adjacent
    entries share a slug-prefix family AND share the same
    spec-level shape, the second entry's design-rationale
    header should explicitly call out the structural identity
    so the contributor of the third entry (API-0385) is on
    notice that the no-body shape is *not* a slug-prefix
    invariant — API-0385 breaks it. The slug-prefix-is-not-
    shape rule earns its keep on every entry, not just on
    family-boundary entries.
  - **Forward-reference accuracy compounds.** API-0383's
    hand-off comment correctly forecast that API-0385 would
    diverge with an optional `requestBody`/`serverId` (a
    claim re-verified at API-0384 write-time against
    `/settings.reloadTraefik > post`). Forward-references
    that survive multi-entry verification gaps are valuable
    onboarding for the next contributor; they should always
    cite the exact spec path (`paths > <op> > <verb>`) and
    the specific axis (parameter / request-body /
    response-set) that the prediction touches.
  - **Zero-marginal-cost append for minimal-shape POSTs.**
    The harness's POST branch with `len(SampleBody) == 0`
    skips the `Content-Type` and body-round-trip assertions
    (per the `if len(tc.SampleBody) > 0` guard inside
    `runAPICoverageSuccess`). For no-body, no-parameter POSTs
    the entire literal is just five fields plus the comment
    block — the marginal cost of adding a new entry to the
    slice is essentially zero. This keeps the per-entry
    review burden focused on the comment block (which
    captures the spec-re-verification trail and the
    cross-references) rather than on case-specific fixture
    plumbing.

    populating every required field (`name`, `certificateData`,
    `privateKey`, `organizationId`) and every optional sibling
    (`certificateId`, `certificatePath`, `autoRenew`, `serverId`),
    inheriting the `cert-cov-*` fixture-isolation namespace opened

    representative) and API-0061 `certificates-remove` (mutation POST

    arc. The 404 → CodeNotFound slot was reserved for API-0060 by
    API-0058's design header; this entry honours that reservation by
    keeping the harness-default failure leg.

  - The certificates/* tag is a shallow arc (4 stories total —
    `all`/`create`/`one`/`remove`) so the every-optional-populated
    rule plus the canonical 404-reservation-for-`*-one` pattern is
    the entire shape of the tag's coverage. Mirror the bitbucket/*
    arc closed at API-0057 if questions arise.
  - The OpenAPI spec uses `anyOf [<scalar>, null]` rather than
    `nullable: true` for nullable fields. The harness's JSON
    round-trip preserves either branch — pick the populated branch
    by default to exercise the full byte path; the absent / `null`
    branch is implicitly covered by the field being optional.
  - Body-only POSTs (no path/query parameters) are the simplest
    POST shape — no `SamplePathParams` / `SampleQuery` literal is
    needed; the harness's POST branch picks up the body via
    `--input`'s JSON `body` field.

    rationale), API-0061 is a mutating POST expected to revert to
    the harness-default 401 → CodeAuth slot — but per-operation

    that prediction. Preliminary spec inspection at hand-off shows
    a required JSON body and 200/400/401/403/500 responses
    (no 404), matching the canonical mutation response set already
    exercised by API-0059.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The 404 → CodeNotFound failure-leg slot is a per-tag resource
    reserved exclusively for the canonical by-id reader (`*-one`
    GETs). When a tag-arc opener (the `*-all` list-style GET)
    forward-reserves that slot in its design-rationale header, the
    by-id peer must consume it via `FailureStatus:
    http.StatusNotFound` and `FailureCode: yerr.CodeNotFound` on

    declares 401/403 responses. This is now the standing precedent
    across server/* (API-0342), bitbucket/* (API-0055), and
    certificates/* (this entry).

    siblings, the `Sample*` field surface is exhaustively a
    one-key `SampleQuery` map. No `SampleBody` is set (GETs in
    this OpenAPI document never carry a `requestBody`).
  - Per-tag fixture-isolation namespaces are append-only across the
    PRD lifecycle: once `cert-cov-*` is opened by the tag-arc
    opener (API-0058), subsequent peers (API-0059, this entry,
    API-0061) must consume `cert-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` slugs and
    must not back-reference any closed cross-tag namespace
    (`bb-cov-*`, `srv-cov-*`, etc.).

## 2026-05-08 - API-0061
- **Implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for `certificates-remove`
  (POST `/certificates.remove`), the by-id mutating POST that closes out
  the certificates/* tag arc opened by API-0058. Adds the fourth and
  final certificates/* `apiCoverageCase` entry — a single required
  `certificateId` (plain string) request body, empty-object 200 body,
  and the harness-default 401 → CodeAuth failure leg (404 → CodeNotFound
  was reserved for and consumed by API-0060 `certificates-one`, the
  canonical by-id reader; the spec for `/certificates.remove` does not
  declare 404 at all). All five subtests (registry invariants, schema
  reachability, manifest inclusion, success round-trip, representative
  failure) flow through the existing `runAPICoverage*` harness — no
  harness changes required. With this entry the certificates/* tag is
  fully covered (4/4: list-all GET, create POST, by-id GET, by-id
  remove POST).
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended the API-0061 struct
    literal between API-0060 and API-0066, sorted by StoryID.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `API-0061.passes` to `true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` — this entry.
- **Verification run** (from repo root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `goimports` — not installed (documented as missing per the
    Codebase Patterns convention).
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — PASS (all 9 packages).
  - `go test -race ./...` — PASS (all 9 packages).
  - `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser` — not
    installed; documented as missing per the Codebase Patterns
    convention.
- **Results**: All quality gates green. The certificates/* tag-arc
  forecast laid out at API-0058's design-rationale header
  (list-all → create → by-id GET (404 override) → by-id remove
  (default 401)) is now fully realised in the test roster.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - None within this story. Future contributors picking up the next
    priority-3 tag (per the PRD roster, cluster/* — API-0062
    `cluster-addManager` is the highest unpassed entry) open a fresh
    `clu-cov-*` namespace and **must not** back-reference the now-
    closed `cert-cov-*` namespace per the per-tag isolation rule.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **Per-tag forecast pays off.** API-0058's design-rationale header
    forecast all four certificates/* failure-leg slot assignments
    (list-all → harness default; create → harness default; by-id GET
    → 404 override; by-id remove → harness default). Each subsequent
    entry (API-0059, API-0060, API-0061) consumed exactly the
    pre-reserved slot with no re-litigation of the family choice.
    When opening a new tag arc, future contributors should write the
    same forecast at the opener so the per-operation re-verification
    on later peers is just a spec sanity check, not a fresh design
    debate.
  - **Single-required-string-body POST is the cheapest mutation
    shape.** `/certificates.remove` is byte-for-byte identical to
    `/compose.cancelDeployment` (API-0066) modulo the field rename.
    The contract test pattern for any minimal-id POST mutation —
    SampleBody with a single string field, default failure leg, no
    SamplePathParams or SampleQuery — is reusable verbatim.
  - **404 absence in delete mutations.** Many Dokploy `*-remove`
    POST operations (this one, API-0044 `backup-remove`, API-0054
    `bitbucket-remove`, API-0298 `project-remove`, API-0344
    `server-remove`) **do not declare 404** in their response set.
    Even though "delete a thing that doesn't exist" feels like the
    canonical 404 case, the API instead surfaces it as a generic
    400/500 in practice. Per the contract-first rule, the test
    asserts the harness default (401 → CodeAuth) rather than
    inventing a 404 path the spec doesn't sanction. This is the
    standing reminder that the per-tag failure-leg override cohort
    is GET-by-id readers, not delete mutations.
  - **Tag-arc closer hygiene.** When the final entry on a tag arc
    lands, the progress.txt entry should call out the 4/4 (or N/N)
    completion explicitly so the next iteration immediately sees the
    namespace is closed and a fresh `<newtag>-cov-*` namespace must
    be opened.

    `set-cov-reloadTraefik-0385` so the success-leg `Content-Type:
    application/json` and body-round-trip assertions stay meaningful.

  - Failure leg defaults to 401 → CodeAuth (canonical settings/* mutating-POST
    representative; spec declares no 404 on this operation).

  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (+~135 lines, new entry at end of
    `coveredAPIOperations`)
  - `ralph/prd.json` (`passes: false` → `true` for API-0385)
  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this log entry)

- Verification run (all from repo root):

  - `go mod tidy` — clean
  - `go vet ./...` — clean
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass

  - `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`
    not installed locally; documented as missing per project convention.

- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - None for this story. The new entry inherits the existing
    `TestAPICoverage_RegistryInvariants`, `TestAPICoverage_SchemaCommandWorks`,
    `TestAPICoverage_SuccessRoundTrip`, `TestAPICoverage_RepresentativeFailure`,
    and `TestAPICoverage_ManifestListsAllCovered` subtests, all of which pass.

- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The `reload*` slug-prefix sub-roster in settings/* now spans three peers
    (API-0383 reloadRedis, API-0384 reloadServer, API-0385 reloadTraefik) and
    splits across the request-body axis: API-0383/0384 are no-body POSTs,
    API-0385 is the first `reload*` peer with an OPTIONAL body carrying an
    OPTIONAL `serverId`. Adjacent slug-prefix peers cannot be assumed to share
    a wire shape — re-verify against `internal/api/data/openapi.json` per
    operation.
  - For an optional-body POST with a single optional `serverId` field, prefer
    populating the body in `SampleBody` (vs leaving it empty). Populating it
    exercises the JSON serialiser's optional-body branch, keeps the
    `Content-Type: application/json` header assertion meaningful, and
    distinguishes the case from the parameter-free POST cohort that shares
    the same response set. API-0353 `settings-cleanAll` is the canonical
    precedent and the right grep target when authoring the next optional-body
    POST in any tag.
  - The next pending story (API-0386 `settings-saveSSHPrivateKey`) declares
    `requestBody.required = true` — pivots the request-body axis from
    OPTIONAL (this entry) to REQUIRED. Re-verify against
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /settings.saveSSHPrivateKey > post`
    before authoring.

    request-body axis within four consecutive entries:
    API-0383/0384 are no-body POSTs (slug `reload*`), API-0385 is
    optional-body (slug `reload*`), API-0386 is required-body with
    a REQUIRED inner field (slug `save*`). The body axis flipped
    twice in four entries, reaffirming the slug-prefix-is-not-
    shape lesson reasserted at API-0371..API-0385.
  - When a payload field is named after a secret type
    (`sshPrivateKey`, `apiKey`, `token`, `password`), prefer a
    plain fixture-token literal over a real-shaped value. The
    schema's only constraint here is `type: string`, so a real
    OpenSSH-format key would (a) be wasted bytes and (b) risk
    accidental shadowing if the secret-redactor surface is ever
    extended heuristically. The deterministic fixture token
    keeps the wire-round-trip assertion uniquely identifiable
    without producing payloads that look like production
    secrets.

    API-0387 `settings-setupGPU` (POST, `requestBody.required =

    `required` array). The next contributor must re-verify
    against `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /settings.setupGPU
    > post` per the API-0345..API-0386 forward-reference lesson —
    the inner-required-field axis pivots from REQUIRED (this
    entry) to OPTIONAL (API-0387) even though the outer
    `requestBody.required = true` axis is shared.

    outer `requestBody.required = true` axis. API-0387 opens the

    API-0386).

    token `set-cov-setupGPU-0387`. Populating the optional axis
    follows the API-0353 `cleanAll` / API-0385 `reloadTraefik`
    precedent for settings/* operations whose only inner field is
    an optional `serverId`; it exercises the JSON serialiser's
    non-empty body branch and keeps the `Content-Type:
    application/json` header assertion meaningful, distinguishing
    the case on the wire from the no-body POST cohort
    (API-0383/API-0384) and the optional-body POST cohort
    (API-0385).

  - Failure leg defaults to 401 → CodeAuth (canonical settings/*

    end of `coveredAPIOperations`)

  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this log entry)

- Verification run (all from repo root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean
  - `go mod tidy` — clean
  - `go vet ./...` — clean
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass
  - Targeted: `go test ./internal/cli -run TestAPICoverage -count=1`
    confirms all five API-0387 subtests pass
    (RegistryInvariants, SchemaCommandWorks, ManifestListsOperation,
    SuccessRoundTrip, RepresentativeFailure).
  - `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
    `goreleaser` not installed locally; documented as missing per

- Remaining risks / follow-ups:
  - None for this story. The new entry inherits the existing
    `TestAPICoverage_RegistryInvariants`,
    `TestAPICoverage_SchemaCommandWorks`,
    `TestAPICoverage_SuccessRoundTrip`,
    `TestAPICoverage_RepresentativeFailure`, and
    `TestAPICoverage_ManifestListsAllCovered` subtests, all of
    which pass.

- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The settings/* roster has now traversed five consecutive
    body-axis configurations within five entries
    (API-0383/API-0384 are no-body POSTs, API-0385 is optional-
    body, API-0386 is required-body with REQUIRED inner field,
    API-0387 is required-body with OPTIONAL inner field). The
    request-body axis is now a two-dimensional axis: outer
    `requestBody.required` × inner `required` array presence.
    Both dimensions must be re-verified per operation; the slug-
    prefix-is-not-shape rule reasserted at API-0371..API-0386
    extends naturally to "outer-required-is-not-inner-required" —
    a `requestBody.required = true` does NOT imply any inner
    field is required.
  - When the only inner field is an optional `serverId` (and the
    outer body is required), populate it with a fixture token
    rather than emitting `{}`. Emitting `{}` would (a) lose the
    body-forwarding wire assertion, (b) collapse the case into the
    optional-body-empty-payload cohort, and (c) fail to
    distinguish the case from a degenerate no-body round-trip.
    API-0353 `cleanAll` and API-0385 `reloadTraefik` are the
    canonical precedents and the right grep targets when
    authoring future settings/* operations whose only inner field
    is an optional `serverId`.
  - The next pending story is API-0388 `settings-toggleDashboard`
    (POST, `requestBody.required = true`, inner properties
    `enableDashboard` (**boolean**) and `serverId` (string), both
    OPTIONAL — no inner `required` array). The next contributor
    must re-verify against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.toggleDashboard > post`. Three axes pivot:
    inner-property-count (1 → 2), inner-property-type (string-only
    → mixed boolean+string), and slug-prefix (`setup*` →
    `toggle*`). Two axes are shared: outer-body REQUIRED and
    inner-required-field absence (no inner `required` array).
    The boolean inner field is the first non-string inner property
    encountered in the recent settings/* arc; the SampleBody must
    use a JSON `true`/`false` literal rather than a fixture-string
    token for the boolean axis.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0389 settings-toggleRequests
- Added the API-0389 entry to `coveredAPIOperations` in
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` so the registry, schema,
  manifest, success-round-trip, and representative-failure
  contracts all assert against `settings-toggleRequests`.
- Spec re-verified against
  `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /settings.toggleRequests > post`
  (POST, `requestBody.required = true`, single REQUIRED inner
  property `enable: boolean`, zero parameters, responses
  200/400/401/403/500 with no 404). The hand-off comment from
  API-0388 `toggleDashboard` correctly forecast all three pivots:
  inner-property-count (2 → 1), inner-property-type (mixed
  boolean+string → boolean-only), inner-required-field
  (no-inner-required → REQUIRED).
- Fixture body carries `{"enable": true}` because the inner schema
  has `required: ["enable"]` — leaving the field out would
  produce a structurally invalid request, unlike API-0388 where
  the `{}` payload would have been spec-valid.
- Failure-leg uses harness defaults (401 → CodeAuth). 404 is
  unavailable per spec (no by-id semantics on a controller-wide
  feature toggle), and 400 stays reserved for stories whose
  coverage burden uniquely hinges on payload-shape validation.
- Forward-reference to API-0390 `settings-updateDockerCleanup`
  flags three pivots for the next contributor: slug-prefix
  `toggle*` → `update*`, inner-property-count 1 → 2, and
  inner-property-type boolean-only → mixed boolean+string.
- Files changed:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (new coverage case)
  - `ralph/prd.json` (passes flipped to true)
  - `ralph/progress.txt` (this entry)
- Verification run from repo root:
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean
  - `go mod tidy` — clean
  - `go vet ./...` — clean
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass
- Remaining risks or follow-ups: none — API-0389 is the second
  `toggle*` peer in settings/* and the hand-off forecast for
  API-0390 was double-checked against the embedded OpenAPI doc.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When the inner schema declares a `required: ["..."]` array,
    the success-leg fixture *must* populate every required
    field. Compare API-0388 (no inner-required → `{}` would have
    been valid) vs API-0389 (`enable` is required → `{}` would
    fail spec validation). The harness only checks a JSON
    round-trip on the wire, but a future stricter validator
    (or a Dokploy-side reject) would punish a missing required
    field.
  - The `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` fixture-token base remains
    the namespace contract even when the inner schema has no
    string field to carry the slug onto the wire. In that case
    (like API-0389) the slug only appears in the closing
    forward-reference comment; the wire payload is just the
    minimal required shape. This matches the precedent set by
    API-0353/API-0383/API-0384 where the body axis was
    no-body / optional-only / minimal.
  - 400 → CodeInvalidInput is *technically* available for any
    operation whose inner schema declares a `required` array
    (a missing required field would 400), but the catalogue
    convention reserves 400 for stories whose coverage burden
    uniquely hinges on payload-shape validation
    (multi-field cross-validation, conditional `oneOf`/`anyOf`,
    length-bounded strings). A single-required-boolean toggle
    stays on the canonical 401 → CodeAuth representative.

  parameter axis (zero parameters), and the
  response-set axis (200/400/401/403/500, no 404)
  are likely shared, but per-operation re-verification
  remains mandatory.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The `update*` slug-prefix family within settings/*
    starts at API-0390 and runs contiguously through
    API-0397 per PRD ordering
    (`updateDockerCleanup`, `updateLogCleanup`,
    `updateMiddlewareTraefikConfig`, `updateServer`,
    `updateServerIp`, `updateTraefikConfig`,
    `updateTraefikFile`, `updateTraefikPorts`). Eight
    consecutive `update*` peers gives ample
    opportunity for axis pivots; do not assume any
    one entry's shape carries over.
  - When the inner schema declares a string-typed
    property (e.g. `serverId` here), the per-case
    fixture-isolation slug
    (`set-cov-<slug>-<storyID>`) should be carried
    on the wire via that property — restoring the
    API-0386/API-0387/API-0388 convention that
    API-0389 was forced to omit (its inner schema
    had only a `boolean` property). This keeps
    successive fixtures distinguishable on the wire
    even when the structural shape (verb, outer-body
    requirement, response set) is identical.
  - When an OPTIONAL field is structurally
    unnecessary (the inner `required` array does not
    name it) but exercising it improves coverage,
    populate it deliberately and document the
    coverage rationale in the case comment. Here
    `serverId` is OPTIONAL but populated to (a)
    exercise the mixed-type boolean+string
    serialiser branch, (b) surface the per-case slug
    on the wire, and (c) keep the fixture
    distinguishable from a hypothetical degenerate
    boolean-only payload that would otherwise be
    structurally indistinguishable from API-0389's
    wire shape.
---

## 2026-05-08 - API-0391
- **Implemented**: Coverage of Dokploy API operation
  `settings-updateLogCleanup` (POST /settings.updateLogCleanup) under tag
  `settings`. Forty-first entry on the settings/* coverage roster and the
  second `update*` slug-prefix peer (after API-0390 `updateDockerCleanup`).
  The harness drives the success leg (200 / `{}`) and the representative
  failure leg (401 → CodeAuth via the harness default) through the existing
  `coveredAPIOperations` table in `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`,
  which exercises both `yalla api call settings-updateLogCleanup --input
  <file> --json` and the schema/manifest projection for the same operationId.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended a single
    `apiCoverageCase` entry for `API-0391 / settings-updateLogCleanup`.
    `Method = http.MethodPost`, `Path = /settings.updateLogCleanup`,
    `Tag = settings`. `SampleBody` carries the lone REQUIRED inner
    property `cronExpression` (`anyOf: [{type:"string"},{type:"null"}]`)
    as a non-null string of the form
    `"0 3 * * * # set-cov-updateLogCleanup-0391"` — a syntactically
    valid daily-at-3am cron expression with the per-case slug embedded
    as a trailing comment fragment, preserving both the
    fixture-isolation convention (`<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>`) and
    the field's plain-language semantics. `SuccessResponse` is `{}`
    matching the spec's empty-object 200 schema. The case comment
    re-verifies the spec against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.updateLogCleanup > post`, documents the three axes
    that pivot from API-0390 (inner-property-count 2→1,
    inner-property-type mixed-boolean+string → nullable-string-only,
    inner-property-name `enableDockerCleanup`/`serverId`→`cronExpression`)
    and the four axes that carry over (slug-prefix `update*`, verb
    POST, outer-body REQUIRED, inner-required-field present), notes
    the **first appearance of the `anyOf`-flavoured nullable-string
    sub-axis** on the settings/* roster, and forecasts the next case
    `API-0392 settings-updateMiddlewareTraefikConfig`.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US `API-0391.passes = true`.
- **Verification run** (from repository root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean (no files reformatted beyond the new entry).
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass; `internal/cli` runs the
    expanded `TestAPICoverage_*` and manifest-coverage suites green
    (~1.0s wall clock).
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass green under the race
    detector (~2.7s wall clock for `internal/cli`).
- **Tools NOT available locally** (documented per the verification policy):
  `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
  `goreleaser`. The repo-level `scripts/verify.sh` reports each missing
  optional tool with an install hint and never silently skips.
- **Remaining risks or follow-ups**:
  - 400 → CodeInvalidInput is technically reachable here (the inner
    schema declares `required: ["cronExpression"]`), but the harness
    reserves payload-validation failures for stories where 400 is the
    operation's *distinguishing* failure mode. A future story can add
    a focused 400-leg fixture for `updateLogCleanup` if a regression
    surfaces in payload-shape validation specific to nullable-string
    fields.
  - The `null` branch of `cronExpression`'s `anyOf` is structurally
    valid but not exercised here because the harness fixes one
    SampleBody per case. A future story whose primary coverage
    burden is nullable-string round-trip behaviour can add a
    sibling fixture under a new StoryID.
  - The trailing-comment cron-expression slug embedding
    (`"0 3 * * * # set-cov-updateLogCleanup-0391"`) relies on the
    Dokploy server tolerating cron-grammar comments (most
    standard cron parsers do; the Go `robfig/cron` library
    accepts `#`-prefixed comments). The harness only asserts
    that the request reached the server and returned 200 — it
    does not assert that the server semantically validated the
    cron string.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **`anyOf: [{type: "X"}, {type: "null"}]` is JSON-Schema's
    nullability idiom, not a polymorphic union.** When you encounter
    this shape on a Dokploy property, treat it as "type X, but null
    is also accepted" rather than "either an X or some other unknown
    shape." Both `null` and any X-typed value are structurally
    valid; the choice between them is a coverage decision, not a
    structural one. Document the choice in the case comment so a
    future contributor adding a sibling story knows which branch
    is already exercised.
  - **When the lone inner property is a domain-specific
    semi-structured string (cron expression, IP address, URL,
    regex), embedding the per-case slug as a raw bareword is
    semantically misleading.** Prefer a syntactically valid value
    of the field's grammar with the slug embedded as a comment or
    trailing identifier (here: the `# slug` comment fragment on a
    valid cron expression). This preserves both the
    fixture-isolation convention and the field's semantics on the
    wire.
  - **Slug-prefix continuity does not imply axis continuity.**
    API-0390 and API-0391 are both `update*` peers in the same
    sub-roster, but three of their seven primary axes pivot
    (inner-property-count, inner-property-type, inner-property-name).
    The slug-prefix-is-not-shape rule reasserted at API-0371..API-0390
    holds at API-0391: per-operation re-verification against
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json` is mandatory before assuming
    any field carries over from a slug-adjacent peer.
  - **The `anyOf` nullability shape is *not* a 400-distinguishing
    payload-validation case.** A 400-leg fixture remains reserved
    for stories whose coverage burden hinges on multi-field
    cross-validation, conditional `oneOf`/`anyOf` *between distinct
    shapes* (not between a scalar and its null sentinel), or
    length-bounded strings. A simple nullable-string field falls
    under the routine settings/* coverage cohort where 401 →
    CodeAuth is the canonical representative failure.

  (POST /settings.updateServer) via the table-driven
  `apiCoverageCase` entry pattern in
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. The harness
  exercises registry invariants (method/path/tag), the
  raw `yalla api call settings-updateServer --input <file>
  --json` success leg with an httptest.Server fixture,
  the representative auth failure leg (401 → CodeAuth
  via the harness default), the `yalla schema get
  settings-updateServer --json` envelope, and the
  manifest entry — satisfying every PRD acceptance
  criterion (registry presence, api call, schema get,
  --no-input safety, success envelope, failure envelope,
  contract tests, manifest coverage) in one fixture.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended a
    single `apiCoverageCase` entry for `API-0393 /
    settings-updateServer`. `Method = http.MethodPost`,
    `Path = /settings.updateServer`, `Tag = settings`.
    `SampleBody` is **omitted entirely** (no-body POST,
    matching API-0354/0357/0358/0359/0370/0383
    precedent). `SuccessResponse` is also omitted so the
    harness defaults to `{}`. The case comment
    re-verifies the spec against
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.updateServer > post`, documents the
    multi-axis pivot from API-0392, **explicitly
    corrects** the API-0392 hand-off comment which
    incorrectly forecast `requestBody.required = true`
    (the actual spec has no `requestBody` at all),
    enumerates the no-body POST sub-cohort that now
    totals seven entries within settings/*, and
    forecasts the next case `API-0394
    settings-updateServerIp` with explicit warning that
    PRD `requestBody.required = false` is ambiguous
    between "no body at all" and "body declared but
    optional" — only direct spec inspection settles the
    outer-body axis.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — set US `API-0393.passes = true`.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` — this report.
- **Verification run** (from repository root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean (no files reformatted beyond
    the new entry).
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass; `internal/cli`
    runs the expanded `TestAPICoverage_*` and
    manifest-coverage suites green (~1.1s wall clock).
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass green
    under the race detector (~2.8s wall clock for
    `internal/cli`).
- **Tools NOT available locally** (documented per the
  verification policy): `goimports`, `golangci-lint`,
  `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`. The
  repo-level `scripts/verify.sh` reports each missing
  optional tool with an install hint and never silently
  skips.
- **Spec axes verified directly against**
  `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
  /settings.updateServer > post`: POST verb, **no
  `requestBody` field at all**, **no `parameters` array**,
  responses 200/400/401/403/500 (no 404), 200 schema is
  `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`. The spec
  block declares only `operationId`, `tags`, `security`,
  and `responses` — strictly smaller than API-0392's
  spec block.
- **Remaining risks or follow-ups**: none for this
  story. Forward-reference recorded in the new entry's
  hand-off comment for API-0394
  `settings-updateServerIp` — per the slug-prefix-is-
  not-shape rule and the cautionary precedent of the
  API-0392 → API-0393 outer-body pivot, the next
  contributor must inspect the spec directly rather
  than trusting the PRD's `requestBody.required = false`
  flag (which is ambiguous between "no body at all"
  and "body declared but optional with non-empty inner
  schema").
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **The PRD's `requestBody.required` flag is an
    insufficient proxy for the spec's outer-body shape.**
    The PRD records `requestBody.required: false,
    contentTypes: []` for API-0393, which is
    superficially compatible with two structurally
    distinct shapes: (a) no `requestBody` field at all
    in the spec (this entry's case), and (b)
    `requestBody` declared with `required: false` and
    a non-empty inner schema. Only direct inspection
    of `internal/api/data/openapi.json` settles which
    branch applies. The API-0392 hand-off comment's
    forecast that API-0393 would carry
    `requestBody.required = true` was a clean miss in
    the *opposite* direction (forecasting REQUIRED but
    the spec has ABSENT) — the correct mental model is
    that the PRD's `requestBody.required` flag tells
    you *nothing* about whether the spec declares a
    `requestBody` field; it only tells you the
    `requestBody.required` flag's value if the field
    exists. Future contributors must inspect the spec
    *unconditionally* before assuming any outer-body
    shape.
  - **The slug-prefix-is-not-shape rule extends to the
    outer-body axis.** Prior `update*` peers in
    settings/* (API-0390 `updateDockerCleanup`, API-0391
    `updateLogCleanup`, API-0392
    `updateMiddlewareTraefikConfig`) all carry a
    REQUIRED `requestBody` with at least one inner
    property. API-0393 `updateServer` shares the
    `update*` slug prefix but has no `requestBody` at
    all. This is the **first instance of an outer-body
    axis pivot inside a contiguous slug sub-roster**
    on the settings/* tag — prior pivots stayed within
    the inner-property axes (count, type, name,
    required-ness) while keeping the outer body
    REQUIRED. The lesson is that *no* axis is
    structurally privileged: any axis can pivot at any
    boundary, and per-operation re-verification is
    mandatory regardless of slug-prefix continuity.
  - **The no-body POST cohort within settings/* now
    totals seven entries** (API-0354, 0357, 0358, 0359,
    0370, 0383, 0393), spread across four slug prefixes
    (`clean*`, `getUpdateData`, `reload*`, `update*`).
    The cohort is structurally homogeneous: no body, no
    parameters, no 404, success schema `{}` with
    `additionalProperties: false`. This is the largest
    structural sub-cohort on the settings/* tag — and
    the only one that spans four distinct slug prefixes.
    Its growth pattern (one new member every 3-13
    stories) suggests future settings/* `update*` peers
    will likely add to it, but the API-0392 → API-0393
    pivot proves that within-cohort membership is *not*
    predictable from slug prefix.
  - **For no-body POSTs, omit both `SampleBody` and
    `SuccessResponse`** — the harness's
    `if len(tc.SampleBody) > 0` guard inside
    `runAPICoverageSuccess` correctly skips writing a
    request body when `SampleBody` is unset, and
    `SuccessResponse`'s default of `{}` matches the
    canonical settings/* mutating-POST 200 schema.
    Carrying an empty `SampleBody: json.RawMessage(\`{}\`)`
    would silently encode a non-empty request body on
    the wire (the JSON object `{}`) which is
    structurally distinct from a true bodyless POST.
    The API-0383 entry's "Leaving `SuccessResponse`
    unset lets the harness default to `{}`" comment is
    the canonical precedent — follow it for every
    no-body POST going forward.
  - **400 → CodeInvalidInput is structurally
    unavailable for the no-body POST cohort.** Without
    a payload to mis-shape, the operation cannot fail
    server-side payload-shape validation; the spec's
    400 response is reserved for other categories of
    invalid input (e.g. invalid `Authorization` header
    formatting) which the harness does not exercise.
    401 → CodeAuth via the harness default is therefore
    the **only** meaningful representative failure for
    no-body POST entries — there is no story-specific
    coverage burden that would justify overriding
    `FailureStatus` / `FailureCode` to 400.
  - **The body-axis flip-flop within settings/* is now
    eleven entries deep across API-0383..API-0393.**
    The body axis is the most volatile axis on the tag
    — it has flipped at every boundary in this stretch:
    no-body / required-body+inner-required (API-0386) /
    required-body+inner-optional-string-only (API-0387) /
    required-body+inner-optional-mixed (API-0388) /
    required-body+inner-required-boolean (API-0389) /
    required-body+inner-required-mixed (API-0390) /
    required-body+inner-required-nullable-string
    (API-0391) / required-body+inner-required-plain-
    string (API-0392) / no-body (API-0393). Future
    contributors should expect continued body-axis
    volatility within settings/* and per-operation
    re-verification stays mandatory across every
    family transition.
---

- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Structural twins do exist on the settings/*
    roster but require zero-pivot verification on
    every axis.** API-0394 `updateServerIp` is the
    first occurrence on the settings/* roster of an
    entry that pivots from its predecessor on **only
    the inner-property-name axis** (vs API-0392
    `updateMiddlewareTraefikConfig`, skipping the
    API-0393 ABSENT-body intermezzo). Eight
    shape-defining axes match: verb (POST),
    outer-body (REQUIRED), inner-property-count
    (one), inner-property-type (plain string),
    inner-required-field (REQUIRED), parameter (zero),
    response-set (200/400/401/403/500, no 404), and
    200-response-shape (`{}` with
    `additionalProperties: false`). Per-operation
    re-verification stays mandatory even when the
    expected outcome is "twin of a prior entry" — the
    forward-reference lesson reasserted at
    API-0345..API-0393 makes the verification cheap
    and the pivot-detection sharp.
  - **The `update*` slug-stem sub-roster's
    outer-body axis oscillates.** API-0390 (REQ) →
    API-0391 (REQ) → API-0392 (REQ) → API-0393
    (ABSENT) → API-0394 (REQ). This is the first
    documented two-step oscillation within the same
    tag's slug-stem sub-roster. The slug-prefix-is-
    not-shape rule extends to the slug-stem axis:
    even matching `updateServer*` (API-0393 vs
    API-0394) does not pre-determine the outer-body
    axis. The lesson generalises: PRD
    `requestBody.required` is only one of three
    possible outer-body states (REQUIRED / OPTIONAL /
    ABSENT), and only direct inspection of
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json` settles the
    distinction between OPTIONAL (declared with
    `required: false`) and ABSENT (no `requestBody`
    field at all).
  - **Slug-on-the-wire convention scales to plausibly-
    typed string fields.** The `serverIp` field is
    conventionally an IP-address-shaped string but
    the spec declares no `format: ipv4`, no
    `pattern`, and no `examples`, so any non-empty
    string is structurally valid. Embedding the slug
    as the whole value (`"set-cov-updateServerIp-0394"`)
    rather than as a comment fragment on a
    domain-typed value (e.g., `"127.0.0.1
    # set-cov-updateServerIp-0394"` would be invalid
    because comments are not part of JSON) preserves
    the slug-on-the-wire convention without
    introducing dialect-specific syntax. The
    Dokploy server is free to either accept the
    string (the spec contract this fixture
    exercises) or reject it on IP-format grounds (a
    server-side concern outside the spec's
    declarative shape) — the harness only asserts
    spec-level structural validity.
  - **Forty-fourth entry on the settings/* roster
    keeps the per-tag-isolation rule.** No
    back-references to closed `srv-cov-*`,
    `proj-cov-*`, `org-cov-*`, `compose-cov-*`,
    `app-cov-*`, `ai-cov-*`, or any other prior
    tag's namespace — the per-tag isolation rule
    reasserted at API-0335..API-0393 holds. Each
    new settings/* entry uses
    `set-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` and is unique within
    the tag.
---

- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - When a forward-reference header reserves a failure-leg slot for a
    later story (here, API-0062/0063 reserved 404 → CodeNotFound for
    API-0064 as the "most read-shaped cluster/* peer"), honor it.
    Skipping the reservation would force a future contributor to either
    relocate the canonical 404 slot or duplicate the harness-default
    leg, both of which break the forward-reference design contract.
  - The `clu-cov-*` per-tag namespace was opened by API-0062 and is
    held open across all subsequent cluster/* entries; only the

    operation in tag) sometimes write a hand-off note for the next
    tag, mirroring the API-0061 `certificates-remove` precedent.
  - Optional-query GETs with a single `serverId` parameter form a tight
    shape cohort (API-0062/0063/0064). The empty-success body (`{}`
    with `additionalProperties: false`) is shared across all three —
    `SuccessResponse: \`{}\`` keeps the success-leg envelope assertion
    focused on `data.method` / `data.status` rather than payload
    projection.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0065
- **Implemented**: Per-operation contract coverage for `cluster-removeWorker`
  (POST /cluster.removeWorker). Fourth and final cluster/* entry — closes
  the `clu-cov-*` per-tag fixture-isolation namespace opened at API-0062

    `apiCoverageCase` literal between API-0064 and API-0066, fixture
    tokens `clu-cov-removeWorker-0065-node` (nodeId) and
    `clu-cov-removeWorker-0065-server` (serverId) on the required POST
    body. Failure leg unset → harness default 401 → `yerr.CodeAuth`.

  - `go test -race ./...` — PASS (internal/cli 2.767s, all packages
    green).

    `goreleaser` are not installed locally; documented as missing
    rather than silently skipped (per Codebase Patterns).
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**: None for API-0065 itself. The
  `clu-cov-*` namespace is now fully populated and CLOSED — no further
  cluster/* entries are pending in the PRD. Next priority-3 tag is
  destination/* opening at API-0102 `destination-all` with a fresh
  `dest-cov-*` namespace.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Slug-prefix-is-not-shape, restated for cluster/***: even within a
    single tag's roster, three of four cluster/* operations are GETs
    with optional `serverId` and the fourth (this entry) is a POST
    with a required body and NO 404 declared. The `cluster-*` slug did
    not predict the wire shape; the spec must be re-verified per
    operation. This re-asserts the API-0371..API-0385 lesson
    intra-tag, not just cross-tag.
  - **Status-code claims must be backed by the spec**. 404 →
    CodeNotFound was already claimed by API-0064 for cluster/*, but
    even if it had been reserved here, claiming it would have been
    wrong because 404 is not declared in the operation's response set
    — the harness contract assertion would surface the mismatch. When
    a story's spec lacks a declared 404, the failure leg defaults to
    the harness 401 → CodeAuth even if the slug suggests a "remove"
    operation that morally could 404. Spec is ground truth.
  - **Closer-entry hand-off note convention**: the closing entry of a
    per-tag namespace must explicitly name the next priority-3 tag
    that will open a fresh namespace and forbid back-references to
    the now-closed namespace. This entry's hand-off names
    destination/* via API-0102 `destination-all`.

  `update*` slug-prefix shared with API-0390..API-0395 cannot

  inner-required-field axes, and the `File` slug-stem may imply a
  multi-field `path`+`content` shape rather than the single-string
  shape declared here.

    three structurally identical "single REQUIRED plain string"

    API-0395 `traefikConfig`), separated only by the API-0393
    `updateServer` ABSENT-body intermezzo. When the spec confirms
    the same shape, it is appropriate to record a "zero pivots"
    forward-reference confirmation rather than synthesise pivots
    that do not exist — the documentation stays honest about what
    the spec actually declares.

    `updateMiddlewareTraefikConfig` and API-0395
    `updateTraefikConfig`) share the exact same wire-level field
    name `traefikConfig`, reaffirming the Dokploy convention that
    Traefik-configuration mutations on settings/* use the same
    field name irrespective of which Traefik configuration scope
    (middleware-level vs global) they target. This is a
    cross-operation convention worth documenting on subsequent
    `Traefik`-stem peers.
  - The body-axis flip-flop within settings/* is now thirteen
    entries deep (API-0383..API-0395). Per-operation
    re-verification remains mandatory across every family
    transition — the PRD ordering does not constrain the spec's
    declared shape.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0396
- Implemented coverage for `settings-updateTraefikFile`
  (POST /settings.updateTraefikFile) via the table-driven
  `apiCoverageCase` entry pattern in
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. The harness exercises
  registry invariants (method/path/tag), the raw
  `yalla api call settings-updateTraefikFile --input <file> --json`
  success leg with an httptest.Server fixture, the representative
  auth failure leg (401 → CodeAuth via the harness default), the
  `yalla schema get settings-updateTraefikFile --json` envelope,
  and the manifest entry — satisfying every PRD acceptance
  criterion (registry presence, api call, schema get, --no-input
  safety, success envelope, failure envelope, contract tests,
  manifest coverage) in one fixture.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended a single
    `apiCoverageCase` entry for `API-0396 /
    settings-updateTraefikFile`. `Method = http.MethodPost`,
    `Path = /settings.updateTraefikFile`, `Tag = settings`.
    `SampleBody` carries the minimal-valid REQUIRED inner shape
    `{"path": "/etc/dokploy/traefik/set-cov-updateTraefikFile-0396.yml",
    "traefikConfig": "# set-cov-updateTraefikFile-0396\n"}`,
    populating both `path` (REQUIRED, plain string) and
    `traefikConfig` (REQUIRED, plain string) and deliberately
    omitting OPTIONAL `serverId`. The path field embeds the
    fixture slug as the basename and the traefikConfig field
    embeds the slug as a YAML/TOML comment, preserving the
    slug-on-the-wire convention on both REQUIRED string axes.
    `SuccessResponse: \`{}\`` matches the spec's
    `additionalProperties: false` empty-object 200 schema.
    Failure-leg fields are intentionally omitted so the harness
    default (401 → CodeAuth) supplies the representative failure
    leg.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `API-0396.passes` from `false` to
    `true`. 269 of 462 stories remain failing; next pending entry
    by priority is API-0397 `settings-updateTraefikPorts`.
- **Verification run** (from repository root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `goimports -w .` — not installed in this environment; gofmt
    handled formatting; documented in the run log.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — PASS (internal/cli 1.297s, all other
    packages cached PASS).
  - `go test -race ./...` — PASS (internal/cli 2.773s, all other
    packages cached PASS).
  - `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, and
    `goreleaser` — not installed locally; documented as missing
    rather than silently skipped (per Codebase Patterns).
- **Results**: All required quality gates green. The new entry
  exercises the same fixture pipeline as predecessors but
  introduces the **first multi-required-field shape on the
  settings/* `update*` sub-roster** (two REQUIRED strings + one
  OPTIONAL string), so the inner-property-count and
  inner-required-field axes pivot decisively from API-0395.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**: None for API-0396 itself.
  The next contributor on API-0397 `settings-updateTraefikPorts`
  must re-verify the spec per the forward-reference lesson — the
  `Ports` slug-stem strongly hints at numeric port data which
  would be the **first non-string inner-property-type pivot**
  within the settings/* `update*` sub-roster, but the
  slug-stem-is-not-shape rule (reasserted at API-0371..API-0396)
  forbids assuming any axis carries over even when the slug stem
  matches at `Traefik`.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **The structural-twin streak on the settings/* `update*`
    sub-roster ends at three.** API-0392 / API-0394 / API-0395
    were structurally identical "single REQUIRED plain string"
    shapes (zero-pivot triplet separated only by the API-0393
    ABSENT-body intermezzo). API-0396 breaks the streak with two
    REQUIRED + one OPTIONAL plain strings — confirming that even
    a stable triplet within a single tag's slug-prefix sub-roster
    cannot pre-determine the next entry's inner shape. The
    forward-reference lesson reasserted at API-0345..API-0395
    becomes sharper here: API-0395's hand-off comment
    explicitly forecast a possible `path` + `content` shape and
    the spec confirmed the forecast at the inner-property-count
    axis (one → three) and the inner-required-field axis (one →
    two), even while the verb, outer-body, parameter,
    response-set, and 200-shape axes stayed identical.
  - **First multi-required-field `update*` shape on settings/*.**
    Prior multi-required-field precedent within the settings/*
    tag was API-0386 `settings-saveSSHPrivateKey`
    (`name`/`privateKey` REQUIRED), but that entry sat on the
    `save*` slug-prefix sub-roster, not `update*`. API-0396 is
    the first `update*` peer requiring more than one inner
    field — useful future signal that the `update*` sub-roster's
    inner-required-field axis is **non-monotonic** (single-field
    at API-0390/0391/0392/0394/0395, multi-field at 0396), so
    no `update*`-prefix-wide invariant can be claimed.
  - **`serverId`-as-OPTIONAL is a settings/* convention worth
    flagging.** API-0394 `settings-updateServerIp`'s `serverId`
    was the entire payload (REQUIRED in that op's local
    semantics), whereas API-0396 declares `serverId` OPTIONAL
    alongside two REQUIRED file-mutation fields. The Dokploy
    convention surfacing here: when `serverId` accompanies
    OTHER REQUIRED fields, it scopes the operation to a remote
    Dokploy worker server (and absent → local Dokploy server);
    when `serverId` IS the operation (e.g. `updateServerIp`),
    its presence/required-status flips. Future settings/*
    entries with multi-field shapes carrying `serverId` should
    inherit this convention check.
  - **Path-shaped string fixture choice.** The spec declares
    `path` as a plain string with no `format`, `pattern`, or
    `examples`, so any non-empty string is structurally valid.
    Embedding the slug as the **basename** of an absolute
    filesystem path (e.g.
    `/etc/dokploy/traefik/set-cov-updateTraefikFile-0396.yml`)
    rather than as a comment fragment preserves the
    slug-on-the-wire convention without contaminating the field's
    conventional semantics. Mirrors the API-0394 `serverIp`
    fixture choice (slug-as-whole-string) where the field's
    conventional shape did not admit an in-band comment syntax.
  - **OPTIONAL-field omission as deliberate coverage choice.**
    This is the first entry on the settings/* roster where the
    inner schema has both REQUIRED and OPTIONAL plain-string
    fields. The fixture deliberately omits the OPTIONAL
    `serverId` so the success-leg exercise stays focused on the
    structurally-minimal valid payload (REQUIRED fields populated,
    OPTIONAL fields absent). Populating the OPTIONAL field would
    have shifted the fixture toward an orthogonal "scoped to
    remote worker" code path which is not part of this story's
    acceptance criteria. Future stories with OPTIONAL fields
    should adopt the same default unless the OPTIONAL field is
    the operation's *distinguishing* coverage axis.

  - go mod tidy (no changes)
  - go vet ./... (clean)
  - go test ./... (all packages pass)
  - go test -race ./... (all packages pass)

  - The `destination/*` roster continues at API-0104 `destination-one` (GET with required `destinationId` query and 404 response — opens the canonical `*-one` 404→CodeNotFound slot for the `dest-cov-*` namespace).
- Learnings for future iterations:
  - The `coveredAPIOperations` registry is append-only and ordered by PRD priority/tag. New entries slot in directly before the next-priority opener (e.g. before API-0246 `organization-active` the priority-3 destination/* group was opened).
  - Multi-field create POSTs in this codebase reliably reuse the `application-create` (API-0014) and `compose-create` (API-0069) precedent: a string-branch fixture for `anyOf [string, null]` fields plus deterministic `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` literal token bases.   - The `dst-cov-*` token shape inside backup/* `destinationId` payloads is a *value* from the backup tag namespace; the destination/* tag namespace itself uses `dest-cov-*`. These prefixes look similar but the per-tag isolation rule keeps them strictly orthogonal.

The harness contract requires the failure status to be a declared response, so 401 → CodeAuth (default) is the only safe representative until a richer-payload destination/* story (e.g. API-0107 `destination-update` or API-0106 `destination-testConnection`) takes the canonical 400 → CodeInvalidInput slot.
