  (POST /settings.updateTraefikPorts).
- Files changed:
  - internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go — appended `apiCoverageCase`
    literal for API-0397 immediately after API-0396. Inner-axis pivot
    versus the prior settings/* `update*` peer: the body is
    `{additionalPorts: [{targetPort:number, publishedPort:number,
    protocol: "tcp"|"udp"|"sctp"}], serverId?: string}` — the **first
    array-of-objects body field on the entire settings/* roster** and
    the first non-string outer-property-type pivot within the
    settings/* `update*` sub-roster. `additionalPorts` REQUIRED;
    `serverId` OPTIONAL. The fixture provides a structurally-minimal
    one-element array carrying a fully-populated port-object
    (`targetPort:8443, publishedPort:8443, protocol:"tcp"`) and omits
    the OPTIONAL outer `serverId`. Failure-leg fields intentionally
    omitted so the harness default (401 → CodeAuth) supplies the
    representative failure leg (404 not declared on this op; 400 is
    technically available but reserved for stories where payload
    validation is the *uniquely* distinguishing failure mode).
  - ralph/prd.json — flipped `API-0397.passes` from `false` to `true`.
    266 of 462 stories remain failing; next pending entry by priority
    is API-0398 `settings-updateWebServerTraefikConfig`.
- Verification run (from repository root):
  - `gofmt -w .` — clean.
  - `goimports -w .` — not installed in this environment; gofmt
    handled formatting; documented as missing rather than silently
    skipped.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./...` — all packages pass (internal/cli 1.308s).
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages pass (internal/cli 2.776s).
  - `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser` — not
    installed locally; documented rather than silently skipped.
- Results: registry, schema, manifest, success-leg, and
  representative-failure assertions all green for
  `settings-updateTraefikPorts` via the existing `TestAPICoverage_*`
  harness. `expectedOperationCount = 450` in
  `internal/api/registry_test.go` already includes this op (the
  count is locked against the embedded openapi.json, not the
  coverage test).
- Remaining risks or follow-ups:
  - The settings/* `update*` sub-roster continues at API-0398
    `settings-updateWebServerTraefikConfig` (POST with required body).
    Per the slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson reasserted at
    API-0371..API-0397, sharing the `update*` slug prefix and the
    `Traefik` slug-stem with API-0392/API-0395/API-0396/API-0397 does
    NOT pre-determine the inner-property-count, inner-property-type,
    or inner-required-field axes — the next contributor MUST
    re-verify against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
    /settings.updateWebServerTraefikConfig > post` before assuming
    any field carries over. `updateWebServerTraefikConfig` may declare
    a single-string-content shape (matching API-0395
    `updateTraefikConfig`), a multi-string shape (matching API-0396
    `updateTraefikFile`), an array-of-objects shape (matching this
    entry), or an entirely orthogonal shape.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Array-of-objects body fields are coverage-axis-distinct from
    scalar-only bodies.** The fixture exercises the inner-item schema
    by populating one fully-formed item (every REQUIRED inner-inner
    field set, no OPTIONAL ones). Multi-element arrays would shift
    coverage toward an orthogonal "multi-port-mapping" axis that is
    not part of the per-operation acceptance criteria.
  - **Slug-on-the-wire convention does NOT apply to numeric or
    enum-bounded fields.** The `set-cov-updateTraefikPorts-0397`
    fixture-token base is established for namespace tracking but
    cannot be embedded in the body anywhere because every inner-inner
    field is `number` or `string`-enum-constrained. The convention
    only applies to free-form string fields (cf. the precedent across
    API-0386/API-0387/API-0388/API-0390/API-0391/API-0392/API-0394/
    API-0395/API-0396 where `path`-shaped or YAML-content-shaped
    free-form strings carry the slug).
  - **Enum-constrained string fields must take a value from the enum
    list.** The `protocol` field's `enum: ["tcp", "udp", "sctp"]`
    means the fixture must pick one of those literal values; using a
    slug-embedded string like `set-cov-updateTraefikPorts-0397` would
    fail enum validation server-side.
  - **`number`-typed fields accept integers in JSON.** Both
    `targetPort` and `publishedPort` are typed `number` (not
    `integer`) in the spec, and JSON-RFC-8259 number literals like
    `8443` parse cleanly into either. The fixture uses bare integers
    rather than fractional notation to match the conventional port-
    number on-the-wire shape.
  - **Body-axis flip-flop now fifteen entries deep within settings/*.**
    The continuous flip-flop across API-0383..API-0397 reaffirms that
    per-operation re-verification is mandatory across every family
    transition; no body-axis assumption can be safely inherited even
    within a single tag.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0398
- Implemented coverage for `settings-updateWebServerTraefikConfig`
  (POST `/settings.updateWebServerTraefikConfig`) by appending a
  single struct entry to `coveredAPIOperations` in
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. The harness automatically
  exercises registry presence, schema reachability, manifest inclusion,
  the success round-trip via httptest, and the representative failure
  leg (401 → CodeAuth via the harness default).
- Files changed:
    * `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` (one new struct literal,
      forty-eighth entry on the settings/* roster).
    * `ralph/prd.json` (`passes: true` on the API-0398 story).
    * `ralph/progress.txt` (this entry).
- Verification run from repo root:
    * `gofmt -w .` — clean.
    * `go mod tidy` — no changes.
    * `go vet ./...` — clean.
    * `go test ./...` — PASS (internal/cli `1.270s`, all other
      packages cached PASS).
    * `go test -race ./...` — PASS (internal/cli `2.765s`, others
      cached PASS).
    * `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`,
      `goreleaser` — not installed locally; documented as missing
      rather than skipped silently per the project convention.
- Spec re-verification (per the forward-reference lesson reasserted
  at API-0345..API-0397) against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
  /settings.updateWebServerTraefikConfig > post`:
    * Method **POST**, tag **settings**, **zero parameters**.
    * `requestBody.required = true`, content-type
      `application/json`, schema `{type: "object", properties:
      {traefikConfig: {type: "string"}}, required: ["traefikConfig"]}`
      — a **single REQUIRED plain-string outer property**.
    * Responses **200/400/401/403/500**, **no 404**. The 200 schema
      is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`, matching every
      prior covered settings/* peer.
- Shape collision with API-0395 `settings-updateTraefikConfig`. On
  all eight shape-defining axes (verb, outer-body required-bit,
  outer-property-count, outer-property-type, outer-required-field-
  count, parameter-count, response-set, 200-shape) this entry is
  identical to API-0395. The only discriminator is the operationId
  itself — the `WebServer` slug-segment narrows the operation to the
  web-server-flavoured Traefik instance rather than the edge-router-
  flavoured Traefik instance addressed by API-0395, but the JSON
  schema layer treats both identically. This is the second
  shape-collision precedent within the settings/* `update*`
  sub-roster (the first was API-0392 / API-0395 sharing aspects of
  the single-required-string body); it does NOT relax the per-
  operation re-verification rule — the spec is still the authority,
  and slug-stem `Traefik` (shared with API-0392/API-0395/API-0396/
  API-0397) does not pre-determine shape (cf. API-0397's array-of-
  objects pivot away from API-0396's multi-string shape).
- Forty-eighth entry on the settings/* roster, inheriting the
  `set-cov-*` per-tag fixture-isolation namespace established by
  API-0351 and extended through API-0397. Per-case fixture token
  `set-cov-updateWebServerTraefikConfig-0398` is unique within the
  `set-cov-*` namespace and orthogonal to every prior tag's
  namespace.
- Fixture body: `{"traefikConfig": "set-cov-updateWebServerTraefik
  Config-0398"}`. The slug-on-the-wire convention applies cleanly
  here because `traefikConfig` is a free-form plain string with no
  enum, format, length, or pattern constraint — restoring the
  convention temporarily suspended at API-0397 where every inner-
  inner field was numeric or enum-bounded.
- Failure leg: harness default (401 → CodeAuth). The spec does not
  declare 404, and the operation has no by-id resource lookup or
  optional `serverId` scoping that could resolve to a missing
  worker. 400 → CodeInvalidInput is technically available (a missing
  `traefikConfig` would fail server-side validation) but reserved
  per the precedent at API-0395..API-0397 for stories where payload
  validation is the operation's *uniquely distinguishing* failure
  mode.
- Forward reference: the next case in the settings/* roster per PRD
  ordering is API-0399 `settings-writeTraefikEnv` (declared a POST
  with `requestBody.required = true`). The next contributor must
  re-verify against `internal/api/data/openapi.json >
  /settings.writeTraefikEnv > post` before assuming any field
  carries over. `writeTraefikEnv` exits the `update*` slug-prefix
  but stays on the `Traefik` slug-stem; per the slug-stem-is-not-
  shape lesson it may declare a single-string shape (matching this
  entry / API-0395), a multi-string shape (matching API-0396), an
  array-of-objects shape (matching API-0397), or an orthogonal
  key/value-pair shape consistent with the `Env` slug-suffix
  conventionally addressing environment-variable data.
- API-0399 will close out the settings/* roster (forty-ninth and
  final entry); after that the user/* roster opens at API-0427 with
  `user-all`.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Shape-collision with a prior peer is a permitted outcome, not
    a violation of slug-stem-is-not-shape.** API-0398 collides with
    API-0395 on every shape-defining axis because the spec authors
    chose to use the same single-string body for both Traefik
    config update operations (one for the edge router, one for the
    web server). The slug-stem-is-not-shape rule says the
    contributor cannot ASSUME shape inheritance; it does not say
    the spec cannot DECLARE shape collision. The contributor still
    re-verifies the spec; if collision occurs, the `// Forward-
    reference confirmation` comment names the colliding peer
    explicitly and lists the matching axes so the next reader can
    diff cleanly.
  - **Slug-on-the-wire convention restored after the API-0397
    suspension.** API-0397's body had only numeric and enum-bounded
    inner fields, so the slug could not be embedded. API-0398's
    single free-form string field restores the convention. Future
    peers should expect the convention to apply field-by-field
    based on type, not entry-by-entry.
  - **No OPTIONAL outer fields → no carve-out comment needed.**
    Unlike API-0396/API-0397 which both carry an OPTIONAL `serverId`
    and required a "deliberately omitted" carve-out, API-0398 has
    only the single REQUIRED `traefikConfig` field. The
    structurally-minimal valid body and the maximal valid body are
    identical, so the fixture-conventions section is shorter by one
    bullet.
  - **Body-axis flip-flop now sixteen entries deep within
    settings/*.** The continuous flip-flop across API-0383..API-0398
    reaffirms that per-operation re-verification is mandatory; the
    shape-collision with API-0395 is the first repeat-shape entry in
    the settings/* `update*` sub-roster but does NOT establish a
    pattern — the next entry (API-0399 `writeTraefikEnv`) must still
    be re-verified per the slug-prefix-is-not-shape rule.

- Verification run: gofmt -w, go mod tidy, go vet ./..., go test ./..., go test -race ./...

- Remaining risks: none specific to this entry — fixture follows the parameter-free-GET precedent established at API-0006 `ai-getAll` and API-0051 `bitbucket-bitbucketProviders`, and the harness-default 401→CodeAuth failure leg.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `user.all` is a parameter-free GET — no request body, no query, no path params. The spec declares 200/400/401/403/404/500 in the response set but only 401 is exercisable from the CLI: 400 has no input axis to fail on, and 404 is reserved for the canonical `*-one` peer (API-0433 `user-get`) per the per-tag-opener convention reasserted at API-0335..API-0399.

  - Slug-on-the-wire convention does NOT apply to parameter-free GETs because there is no string field to embed the sentinel into. The slug lives only in the fixture comment as the namespace anchor; that is sufficient for `git grep`-traceability and per-test isolation, since the harness asserts the wire-level invariants (method, path, Authorization header, empty query string, no body) without requiring any per-case body bytes.
  - List-shaped `*-all` GETs that declare 404 in the spec are an unusual property worth noting — most Dokploy `*-all` endpoints return an empty array on absent results rather than 404. The 404-in-spec declaration here does not unlock 404→CodeNotFound coverage at this entry; it remains reserved for the by-id `*-one` peer per the per-tag opener convention.
  - The cross-tag transition from settings/* to user/* nullifies every inheritable axis per the slug-prefix-is-not-shape and slug-stem-is-not-shape lessons. Even though the prior tag's terminal entry (API-0399 `settings-writeTraefikEnv`) was a POST with a one-required-string + one-optional-string body, the user/* opener is a parameter-free GET — confirming once more that PRD ordering is not shape-preserving across tag boundaries.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0428
- Implemented Cover user-assignPermissions (POST /user.assignPermissions)

- Remaining risks: none specific to this entry — fixture follows the multi-required-field-body precedent and the harness-default 401→CodeAuth failure leg.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `user.assignPermissions` is the first non-trivial multi-field shape in the user/* roster. Fifteen REQUIRED outer properties: one plain string (`id`), three array-of-strings (`accessedProjects`, `accessedEnvironments`, `accessedServices`), and **eleven REQUIRED booleans** (`canCreateProjects`, `canCreateServices`, `canDeleteProjects`, `canDeleteServices`, `canAccessToDocker`, `canAccessToTraefikFiles`, `canAccessToAPI`, `canAccessToSSHKeys`, `canAccessToGitProviders`, `canDeleteEnvironments`, `canCreateEnvironments`). No OPTIONAL outer fields. This is the highest required-field count seen on a single Dokploy operation in this catalogue so far; the fixture mixes true/false values across the eleven booleans rather than collapsing to all-true so the encoded payload remains non-trivially distinguishable.

Future user/* peers carrying array-of-strings fields should follow this convention so the success-leg body assertion covers both scalar and array-element string content.
  - Spec declares 200/400/401/403/500 — **no 404**. Permission mutations are scoped by the body's `id` field rather than a path parameter, so the by-id resource lookup that would justify 404 is absent. The canonical `*-one` peer (API-0433 `user-get`) remains the reserved 404 → CodeNotFound carrier within the user/* roster.
  - The slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson holds even within the same tag: API-0427 `user-all` (parameter-free GET) and this entry (POST with fifteen required body fields) share the `user-` operation-stem but have entirely orthogonal shape signatures. Future user/* contributors must continue per-operation re-verification.
  - Single-required-string slug fields accept the sentinel `usr-cov-assignPermissions-0428` directly (no length, pattern, or format constraint on `id`); array-of-strings fields embed the same sentinel as the lone element so the per-case fixture token base remains uniformly traceable across every populated string surface.

    (API-0428) or parameter-only (API-0427/0429). API-0430 establishes the
    convention: when the spec marks only a subset of body properties
    REQUIRED, the fixture populates only those required fields and leaves
    every optional knob unset. This keeps the success-leg assertion
    focused on the required-field forwarding contract and avoids
    accidentally codifying the optional-field default behaviour as
    contract.
  - `metadata` declared as `type: object` with no `properties` is a
    free-form bag — any JSON object structurally satisfies the contract.
    The fixture supplies a single-key object whose value carries the
    same per-case sentinel so the slug-on-the-wire token base remains
    traceable across every populated string surface.
  - The slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson holds even within the same tag:
    among user/* alone we now have a parameter-free GET (API-0427), a
    fully-required-body POST (API-0428), a single-required-query GET
    (API-0429), and a partially-required-body POST (API-0430) — four
    orthogonal shape signatures sharing the `user-` operation-stem.
    Future user/* contributors must continue per-operation re-verification.
  - **404-absence cluster within user/* POSTs.** Both API-0428
    `user-assignPermissions` and API-0430 `user-createApiKey` declare
    response codes 200/400/401/403/500 with no 404. The structural
    rationale is the same: neither operation performs a by-id lookup
    before its write (assignPermissions scopes by body's `id` rather
    than path; createApiKey mints a fresh resource), so CodeNotFound is
    not an applicable failure representative for either entry.
---

- All five harness assertions for API-0104 pass via the
  `coveredAPIOperations` fixture: registry presence (method/path/tag
  invariants), schema reachability through `yalla schema get
  destination-one --json`, manifest inclusion, success exchange
  (httptest with `destinationId` query forwarded by the CLI through
  `runAPICoverageSuccess` and re-read on the server), and the 404 →
  CodeNotFound failure path through `runAPICoverageFailure`.

Remaining risks or follow-ups:
- 400 → CodeInvalidInput coverage for query-shape validation on by-id
  GETs is still pending across the destination/* roster; the harness
  here only proves required-query forwarding plus the 404 override and
  the cross-tag 401 default still applies on parameter-free / mutation
  peers.
- The destination/* roster still has API-0105 (`destination-remove`),
  API-0106 (`destination-testConnection`), and API-0107
  (`destination-update`) outstanding before the namespace can close.
- The `dest-cov-*` namespace is now consumed by three distinct slug
  bases (`dest-cov-all-*` implicit at API-0102, `dest-cov-create-*` at
  API-0103, `dest-cov-one-*` here at API-0104). Future destination/*
  contributors must continue picking fresh slugs to avoid cross-story
  fixture collisions.

Learnings for future iterations:
  - **Canonical *-one slot 404 → CodeNotFound override pattern.** When
    an opener's failure-leg discussion *explicitly reserves* the 404
    slot for the upcoming `*-one` by-id peer (as API-0102 did here),
    that peer must claim it via `FailureStatus: http.StatusNotFound`
    + `FailureCode: yerr.CodeNotFound`. Leaving the harness default
    (401 → CodeAuth) in place would silently break the cross-tag
    convention that API-0055 / API-0060 / API-0342 already encode.
    Note that not every tag follows this — API-0251 `organization-one`
    intentionally kept the harness default per its own design-rationale
    block. Always read the opener's forecast before deciding.
  - **By-id GET wire shape is the most-replicated shape signature in
    the harness.** API-0104 makes seven covered peers
    (`ai-one`, `application-one`, `bitbucket-one`, `certificates-one`,
    `organization-one`, `server-one`, `destination-one`) sharing the
    exact same wire convention: GET, no body, exactly one required
    `<resourceId>` query param. The slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson
    still demands per-operation re-verification, but contributors can
    safely copy the SampleQuery skeleton and only swap the param name.
  - **Single-required-query-param GETs need only `SampleQuery`.**
    Leaving SamplePathParams and SampleBody unset is the correct
    minimal envelope; `buildCoverageInputArgs` writes only the present
    fields, and `runAPICoverageSuccess` re-reads `r.URL.Query()` to
    confirm propagation. Anything else would conflate the contract.

    (raw API call + schema + manifest) require **no production code
    changes** — only a single `apiCoverageCase` literal in
    `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. The shared harness handles
    every acceptance bullet automatically.

    open namespace must not back-reference any closed sibling-tag
    namespace, but it **must** stay collision-free against earlier

## 2026-05-08 - API-0432 - Cover user-generateToken (POST /user.generateToken)

What was implemented:
- Appended a new `apiCoverageCase` struct literal for API-0432
  (`user-generateToken`) to `coveredAPIOperations` in
  `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`. Sixth entry on the user/* coverage
  roster, slotted in StoryID order after API-0431 `user-deleteApiKey` and
  immediately before the existing closing `}` of the slice literal.
- Spec re-verified against `internal/api/data/openapi.json
  > /user.generateToken > post`: a POST with **no `requestBody`**
  (omitted entirely from the spec) and **zero declared parameters**
  (no query, no path, no header). Responses 200/400/401/403/500;
  the absence of 404 is consistent with API-0428/0430/0431 — Dokploy's
  user/* mutations do not expose a discrete by-id lookup before the
  write. 200 schema is `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`.
- Structurally novel for user/*: the roster's first **parameter-free
  POST** body shape. Prior user/* entries were either parameter-free
  GET (API-0427), parameter-only GET (API-0429), fully-required POST
  (API-0428), partially-required POST (API-0430), or single-required-
  string POST (API-0431). `SampleBody`, `SampleQuery`, and
  `SamplePathParams` are all left zero — `buildCoverageInputArgs`
  short-circuits to `nil` so the CLI is invoked without `--input`,
  mirroring the parameter-free precedent at API-0427 `user-all`,
  API-0006 `ai-getAll`, and API-0051 `bitbucket-bitbucketProviders`.
- Fixture token base `usr-cov-generateToken-0432` follows the
  `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` convention shared with every prior peer
  and stays orthogonal to every prior tag's per-tag namespace. The
  slug-on-the-wire convention does not apply (no string fields to
  embed the sentinel into); the slug lives only in the comment.
- `SuccessResponse: \`{}\`` mirrors the spec's empty 200 envelope; the
  success-leg envelope assertion focuses on `data.method` /
  `data.status` rather than payload projection.
- Failure-leg fields intentionally omitted: harness defaults
  (`tc.FailureStatus == 0` → 401, `tc.FailureCode == ""` → CodeAuth)
  exercise the 401 → CodeAuth representative. 400 → CodeInvalidInput
  is structurally inapplicable here — with zero parameters and no
  request body the CLI cannot construct a syntactically invalid
  payload, so the spec's defensive 400 declaration is unreachable.
- Flipped `passes: false → true` on the API-0432 entry in
  `ralph/prd.json`.

Files changed:
- internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go (one new struct literal appended after
  API-0431, plus the unrelated forward-reference comment in API-0427
  was already correct from a prior pass)
- ralph/prd.json (passes flipped false → true on API-0432)
- ralph/progress.txt (this entry)

Verification run:
- `gofmt -w .` — clean
- `go mod tidy` — clean
- `go vet ./...` — clean
- `go test ./...` — PASS (all packages)
- `go test -race ./...` — PASS (all packages)
- `go test ./internal/cli -run 'TestAPICoverage|TestManifest' -count=1 -v` —
  observed PASS for `API-0432/user-generateToken` across all four
  coverage subtests (`Success`, `RepresentativeFailure`,
  `RegistryInvariants`, `SchemaCommandWorks`) and the aggregate
  `TestManifest_JSONIncludesEveryAPIOperation`.

Results:
- All required quality checks green. Coverage-roster size grows by one
  entry; no other code paths touched. The harness's existing parameter-
  free branch (`len(tc.SampleBody) > 0` gate) handles the new POST
  cleanly without harness changes.

Remaining risks or follow-ups:
- None for this story. The next priority-2 user/* entry is API-0433
  `user-get` — per the forward-reference lesson the next contributor
  must re-verify the spec against `internal/api/data/openapi.json`
  before assuming any axis carries over from API-0432. Notably,
  `user-get` is the canonical `*-one`-shaped by-id user lookup the
  API-0427 comment forecasts as the natural home for the user/*
  roster's 404 → CodeNotFound representative; the spec re-verification
  must confirm whether the 404 declaration actually lands there.

Learnings for future iterations:
- **Parameter-free POSTs are a real shape category.** Prior user/*
  POSTs all carried request bodies (API-0428/0430/0431). API-0432 is
  the roster's first parameter-free POST: the spec omits `requestBody`
  entirely and declares zero parameters. The harness handles this case
  via `buildCoverageInputArgs` returning `nil` when all three fixture
  axes are empty, so the CLI is invoked without `--input` and the
  success-leg subtest reduces to wire-level invariants. No harness
  change is needed — the parameter-free GET path at API-0427 already
  exercises the same code branch.
- **400 → CodeInvalidInput is structurally unreachable when the spec
  declares zero input axes.** Even though the spec lists 400 in the
  response set, with no parameters and no request body the CLI cannot
  construct a syntactically invalid payload. Recording this as an
  inline comment in the entry keeps the omission discoverable for
  reviewers who might otherwise wonder why `FailureCode: CodeInvalidInput`
  is not exercised. 401 → CodeAuth via the harness default stays the
  most informative representative because authentication is the
  universal failure mode every authenticated POST must re-prove.
- **Cross-roster precedent for parameter-free POSTs is rare.** Most
  prior parameter-free coverage stories were GETs (API-0006 `ai-getAll`,
  API-0051 `bitbucket-bitbucketProviders`, API-0427 `user-all`). When
  modelling future parameter-free POSTs, lean on the API-0432 entry
  for the body-shape narrative rather than on those GET openers — the
  GET cases share the no-fixture pattern but differ on the verb and
  on the harness's body-assertion gate.
- **The forward-reference comment in API-0427 already enumerates
  API-0428..API-0436 by name** including API-0432 `user-generateToken`,
  API-0433 `user-get`, API-0434 `user-getBackups`, etc. Future user/*
  iterations can confirm the next slug at a glance without re-reading
  the PRD index — but the spec axes (parameters, body, responses) must
  always be re-verified per-operation as the slug-stem-is-not-shape
  lesson dictates.

  honoured by re-verifying per-operation, not by inheriting on tag prefix.
- Fixture token base `dest-cov-update-0107` keeps the `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>`
  convention; `name` carries `yalla-coverage-destination-update-0107` so a
  `git grep` traces back to this story; `provider` uses the string variant of
  its `anyOf` schema; `serverId` is populated to keep the optional-axis
  assertion meaningful (mirrors API-0103 / API-0106).
- Failure leg: harness-default 401 → CodeAuth — the spec declares no 404 for
  this operation, so the API-0104 `destination-one` 404 → CodeNotFound override
  does not apply here even though `destinationId` selects a row. Mirrors the
  cross-tag mutation default established by API-0002 / API-0003 / API-0014.
- Files changed: `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go`, `ralph/prd.json`,
  `ralph/progress.txt`.
- Verification: `gofmt -w .`, `go mod tidy`, `go vet ./...`,
  `go test ./... -count=1`, `go test -race ./... -count=1` — all pass.
  `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, and `goreleaser`
  are not installed locally and were skipped (documented baseline).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `destination-update` is the by-id mutation peer of `destination-create` /
    `destination-testConnection`: identical S3-credential field set plus a
    required `destinationId` selector; the `additionalProperties:false`
    empty-object 200 schema is preserved across the destination/* mutation
    cohort. Future mutate-by-id stories should follow this peer pattern
    (deterministic `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` selector, not a UUID, so a
    missing-id 404 cannot be mis-attributed to malformed UUID validation).
  - The destination/* tag roster closes at API-0107 in the contiguous PRD
    block; API-0108 onward jumps to the docker/* tag, so the next
    destination-tag case must re-verify the spec from
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json` per the forward-reference lesson before
    assuming any field shape is identical to this entry.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0434
- **Implemented**: Coverage entry for the Dokploy API operation
  `user-getBackups` (GET /user.getBackups). The operation is structurally
  identical to API-0427 `user-all` and API-0433 `user-get`: a parameter-
  free GET with no request body, no path/query/header parameters, and a
  200 schema of `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`. Wiring is
  therefore a single struct-literal append to `coveredAPIOperations` —
  the existing harness covers registry invariants, schema reachability,
  manifest inclusion, success round-trip, and the 401 → CodeAuth
  representative failure leg automatically.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended the API-0434 entry
    after API-0433, sorted by StoryID. Mirrors the parameter-free GET
    cohort with the standard `usr-cov-getBackups-0434` fixture-namespace
    docstring, the per-tag isolation reminder, the "next contributor
    must re-verify against openapi.json" forward-reference lesson, and
    the explanation of why 400/404 are structurally inapplicable for
    this shape (no input the CLI can supply could exercise either —
    they are defensive declarations only).
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped US API-0434 `passes: false` → `true`.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -l .` — clean.
  - `go mod tidy` — clean.
  - `go vet ./...` — clean.
  - `go test ./internal/cli -run TestAPICoverage -count=1` — `ok` (all
    five subtests pass for API-0434: RegistryInvariants,
    SchemaCommandWorks, ManifestListsOperation, SuccessRoundTrip,
    RepresentativeFailure).
  - `go test ./...` — all packages `ok`.
  - `go test -race ./...` — all packages `ok`.
- **Tools NOT available locally** (unchanged): `goimports`, `golangci-lint`,
  `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser`.
- **Remaining risks / follow-ups**:
  - Same defensive-spec note as API-0427 / API-0433: the spec declares
    400 and 404 on this operation but neither is reachable from a
    legitimate `--input` document (zero parameters, no body), so we
    deliberately exercise only the 401 representative — the harness
    enforces this consistency across the parameter-free GET cohort.
  - Next priority-2 user/* story is API-0435 `user-getContainerMetrics`
    — the next contributor must re-verify the openapi.json shape
    rather than assume it inherits this entry's "empty closed-shape
    `{}` --input" pattern, per the slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson
    re-asserted at API-0371..API-0433.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The parameter-free authenticated-GET cohort
    (API-0006/API-0051/API-0204/API-0247/API-0427/API-0433/API-0434) is
    now seven entries strong; future entries in this cohort can copy
    the API-0434 docstring template verbatim, swap the StoryID, slug,
    operation-stem, and cross-references, and confirm the openapi.json
    shape is still `{}` with `additionalProperties: false`. No code
    changes outside `coveredAPIOperations` are required because the
    harness's `len(tc.SampleBody) > 0` short-circuit handles the
    empty-success leg automatically.
  - The "Authorization bearer is the only principal selector"
    discussion in API-0433 carries over verbatim to `user-getBackups`:
    the operation lists *backups owned by the authenticated user*, not
    backups for an arbitrary userId. That is the structural reason
    `--input '{}'` is the canonical agent invocation here and 404 is
    not exercisable.

## 2026-05-08 - API-0108 - Cover docker-getConfig (GET /docker.getConfig)
- **Implemented**: First entry in the docker/* coverage roster. The destination/* tag
  closed at API-0107 `destination-update`; this story opens the docker/* tag and the
  fresh `docker-cov-*` per-tag fixture-isolation namespace. Spec source
  `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /docker.getConfig > get` declares a parameter-only
  GET with two query params: `containerId` (string, REQUIRED — the container whose
  Docker low-level config blob is returned) and `serverId` (string, OPTIONAL —
  scopes to a specific Dokploy worker server). No request body. Responses
  200/400/401/403/404/500 with a `{}` (`additionalProperties:false`) success
  schema. The harness's `runAPICoverageSuccess` re-reads `r.URL.Query()` to confirm
  end-to-end forwarding for both populated params.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended the API-0108 entry between
    API-0107 (`destination-update`, last destination/* peer) and API-0246
    (`organization-active`). Per the established
    `<tag>-cov-<slug>-<storyID>` deterministic-but-clearly-fake convention the
    fixture token is `docker-cov-get-config-0108` and the optional `serverId`
    reuses the cross-tag `server-cov-*` value-axis seen at API-0041
    `backup-listBackupFiles`.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `passes` to `true` for the API-0108 story.
- **Verification run**:
  - `gofmt -w .` → clean.
  - `go mod tidy` → no diff.
  - `go vet ./...` → no diagnostics.
  - `go test ./...` → all packages pass (`internal/cli` 1.118s, others cached).
  - `go test -race ./...` → all packages pass (`internal/cli` 2.849s, others cached).
  - `goimports`, `golangci-lint`, `staticcheck`, `govulncheck`, `goreleaser` are
    not installed locally and were skipped per the existing project documentation.
- **Results**: API-0108 covered. The CLI registry/manifest/schema/success/failure
  invariants all run automatically through the existing `coveredAPIOperations`
  harness — no additional code changes were required outside the struct-literal
  append. Remaining failing stories: 251 (next highest priority is API-0109
  `docker-getContainers`).
- **Remaining risks or follow-ups**:
  - None for this story. The next priority-3 docker/* peer is API-0109
    `docker-getContainers` — per the slug-prefix-is-not-shape lesson the next
    contributor must re-verify the spec at
    `internal/api/data/openapi.json > /docker.getContainers > get` rather than
    assume it inherits this entry's REQUIRED+OPTIONAL shape (per spec scan
    API-0109 has only an optional `serverId` and no REQUIRED parameter, so it
    represents a different sub-shape).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - **Per-tag opener fixture-isolation namespace.** Every first entry in a new
    tag opens a fresh `<tag>-cov-*` namespace. The destination/* tag closed at
    API-0107; API-0108 opens `docker-cov-*`. The closed namespaces (now
    explicitly enumerated in the entry's "must not back-reference" list) are
    `admin-cov-*`, `ai-cov-*`, `app-cov-*`, `backup-cov-*`, `bb-cov-*`,
    `cert-cov-*`, `clu-cov-*`, `compose-cov-*`, `deployment-cov-*`,
    `dest-cov-*`, `org-cov-*`, `proj-cov-*`, `set-cov-*`, `srv-cov-*`. Future
    docker/* peers (API-0109..API-0114) must stay inside `docker-cov-*` and
    add no cross-tag value-axis literals other than the established
    `server-cov-*` (optional `serverId`) and `dst-cov-*` (backup
    `destinationId`) precedents.
  - **REQUIRED + OPTIONAL GET shape precedent.** The closest cross-tag template
    for `docker-getConfig` is API-0077 `compose-getTags` (REQUIRED + OPTIONAL,
    two params) and API-0041 `backup-listBackupFiles` (REQUIRED + REQUIRED +
    OPTIONAL, three params). Both populate the optional axis so the success-leg
    `r.URL.Query()` re-read exercises every declared parameter — the harness
    does not gate on `required`-ness and forwards whatever `SampleQuery`
    carries. Future API-0109..API-0113 docker/* GETs that share this REQUIRED
    + OPTIONAL pattern can copy the API-0108 docstring template verbatim and
    swap the StoryID, slug, parameter names, and per-operation cross-references.
  - **Per-tag opener default failure leg stays at 401 → CodeAuth.** Even though
    `docker.getConfig` declares 404 in its response set (an unresolvable
    `containerId`), the kickoff entry for a new tag exercises the universal
    `E_AUTH` invariant — auth is the single failure mode every authenticated
    Dokploy operation must re-prove before path/row resolution runs. The
    404 → CodeNotFound representative is reserved for a later docker/* peer
    where the spec re-verification confirms 404 is exercisable from a
    legitimate `--input` document. This precedent now holds across every
    per-tag opener (API-0006 ai, API-0021 application, API-0094 deployment,
    API-0246 organization, API-0427 user, API-0108 docker).
  - **Forward-reference comment for the next case is mandatory.** Every entry
    closes with a reminder of the next per-tag PRD slug AND the slug-prefix-is-
    not-shape lesson. API-0109 `docker-getContainers` is the natural next
    docker/* coverage story; its spec lookup is a hard gate before any
    contributor can copy this entry's shape.
---

## 2026-05-08 - API-0435
- **Implemented**: Coverage entry for the Dokploy API operation
  `user-getContainerMetrics` (GET /user.getContainerMetrics). Unlike the
  parameter-free GET cohort that immediately precedes it
  (API-0427/0433/0434), this operation requires four query parameters
  (`url`, `token`, `appName`, `dataPoints`) and ferries an upstream
  bearer token to a remote metrics scrape target. Wiring is therefore a
  single struct-literal append to `coveredAPIOperations` with a populated
  `SampleQuery` map; the existing harness covers registry invariants,
  schema reachability, manifest inclusion, success round-trip (including
  query-string verbatim propagation), and the 401 → CodeAuth
  representative failure leg automatically.
- **Files changed**:
  - `internal/cli/api_coverage_test.go` — appended the API-0435 entry
    after API-0434, sorted by StoryID. Mirrors the multi-query-with-
    upstream-secret precedent from API-0007 `ai-getModels`. Uses the
    `usr-cov-getContainerMetrics-0435` fixture-namespace token for
    `appName` so a regression dropping the param surfaces an
    unmistakable failure string. The placeholder secret
    `fake-metrics-token-coverage-0435` lives only in the per-test JSON
    `--input` document and never reaches stdout/stderr or git history.
  - `ralph/prd.json` — flipped `passes` to `true` for API-0435.
  - `ralph/progress.txt` — this entry.
- **Verification**:
  - `gofmt -w .` (clean)
  - `go mod tidy` (no changes)
  - `go vet ./...` (clean)
  - `go test ./...` (all packages pass)
  - `go test -race ./...` (all packages pass)
- **Remaining risks**: None directly. The 400/404 failure-leg slots are
  declared in the spec but exercising them would require driving the
  remote metrics endpoint into a specific failure shape — outside the
  httptest-bounded contract scope. The 401 → CodeAuth representative
  remains the canonical cohort-wide failure leg.
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The `usr-cov-getContainerMetrics-0435` fixture sentinel is wired
    onto the wire via the `appName` query param; the
    slug-on-the-wire convention applies here because the operation
    declares string parameters the fixture can embed the sentinel
    into. Contrast with API-0434 where the slug-on-the-wire
    convention did *not* apply because the op was parameter-free.
  - `dataPoints` is declared as a string type in the OpenAPI spec
    even though it semantically represents an integer count; the
    fixture supplies `"50"` (a numeric string) to match the spec's
    wire shape literally — coercing to `50` (number) would violate
    the closed-shape contract.
  - The harness's `output.NewRedactor` only redacts the global
    `--token` flag; per-operation query-string secrets like the
    metrics-target `token` are not redacted in CLI logs or error
    messages. Future contributors covering similar operations
    (upstream provider tokens passed as query params) should note
    this defence-in-depth gap.
---
